# Procedure Registry

Status of This Memo

This document is the enumeration half of a pair. It defines what a Core Operation row is and
lists every one of them. `gaia-conformance/docs/registry-completeness-checks.md` defines the
test, and `gaia-conformance/docs/check-registry.mjs` runs it. The registry is complete when
that script exits zero; completeness is an execution result here, never a claim.

## 1. Where this document comes from

The specification at `GaiaSekkeiShiyousho_v32.md` is not a specification of operations. It is
a description of the space Gaia intends to produce: who relates to whom, under what evidence,
toward what end. Line 14173 asserts that a `Procedure Registry` exists and fixes the twenty
fields of a row, but supplies no row; line 14175 demands that every state-changing procedure in
the body correspond to exactly one row, without listing them. The rows below are therefore
derived, and three things about the derivation matter.

First, the derivation is a reading, not an extraction. The specification states its rules and
the operations follow from them only once someone decides which actor is the subject, which
object is the target, and which contract binds them. The reader who wants to check the
derivation checks the cited evidence, which every row carries.

Second, every name the specification itself gives is kept verbatim: `CreateAssetRecord`,
`ProcessPaymentCallback`, `SoulTransferFinalize`, `ForumRootSuccession`,
`StorageAuditResponse`, `SubmitComputeResult`, `CreateServiceOrder` at 14181;
`IssueCertificate`, `CreateCheckpoint`, `PublishAsset`, `UpdateAsset`,
`RevokeOrGrantContentAccess`, `CreateCivicVote`, `CreateCivicNeedAsset`, `ClaimPayout`,
`ChangePayoutBeneficiary`, `CreateNewTransfer`, `CreateAuthorityDelegation`,
`CreateForum`, `CreateRootEntry` at 6667..6679; `SynchronizeClock`,
`RequestTimeAttestation`, `RespondToTimeChallenge`, `RequestRevalidation`,
`SubmitRecoveryProof`, `FetchRecoveryMaterial`, `ReceiveReinstatementProof` at 6023..6035;
`SubmitTransferDispute`, `SubmitTransferResolutionEvidence`,
`FinalizeTransferWhenAuthorized` at 6657..6661. Where the specification gives no name, the row
uses Verb+Object PascalCase.

Third, the ten Core Operation framework kinds keep the SCREAMING_SNAKE spelling that
`gaia-operation/RFC.md` already declares, because renaming them would force a cross-directory
edit for no semantic gain. The registry therefore carries two naming styles, and that is a
decision of this document with an override condition: if `gaia-operation/RFC.md` is revised to
PascalCase, this registry follows in the same revision.

## 2. The row

The twenty fields are the specification's own, verbatim from line 14173:

| # | field | type |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `kind` | the canonical CoreOperationKind identifier |
| 2 | `purpose` | one sentence naming whose purpose the operation serves |
| 3 | `mutates_protocol_state` | bool |
| 4 | `required_authority` | from the authority vocabulary of the checks document |
| 5 | `requires_remote_time_handshake` | bool |
| 6 | `requires_session_binding` | bool |
| 7 | `required_pre_state_checkpoint` | bool |
| 8 | `required_proof_classes` | §22.3 claim identifiers |
| 9 | `idempotency_scope` | the identity tuple that makes two requests one |
| 10 | `initial_status` | one OperationStatus |
| 11 | `allowed_status_transitions` | status to permitted successors |
| 12 | `external_dependency_class` | from the vocabulary of the checks document |
| 13 | `result_object_classes` | canonical object types produced or accepted |
| 14 | `finality_predicate` | what makes the operation final, and which conservation law binds it |
| 15 | `cancellation_rule` | when cancellation is still possible |
| 16 | `reversal_or_recovery_rule` | how reversal and recovery proceed |
| 17 | `rest` | method and path |
| 18 | `websocket` | one of the ten methods at 14159 |
| 19 | `cli` | one of the commands at 14161 |
| 20 | `reject_code_families` | the thirteen families of §22.2 |

Two fields are carried for the checks and are not part of the twenty:

| field | meaning |
|---|---|
| `disposition` | active, framework or excluded |
| `evidence` | the specification lines that establish the row, and any note |
| `position` | the enumerated sequence the row is a step in, or `unsequenced` |

### 2.3 Sequence position, and the number that matters

The specification enumerates sequences in eight places: §8 at 3885..3974, §10.10 at
4578..4618, §13.11 at 5551..5568, §16.7 at 6541..6800, §18.17..18.19 at 8714..8951,
§23.11..23.16 at 12060..12422, the Remote Session Establishment procedure at 14117..14126, and
§29.3 at 15847..15865. Together with the thirty seam scenarios at §25.2, those are every
sequence the specification states.

**Of the 185 active and framework rows, 67 are placed in an enumerated sequence, 86 are single-step
operations, and 32 are operations whose procedure this design supplies.**

The three numbers moved because the first two classes were over-counted, not because the
specification changed. An operation counted as placed was placed in an entry that named it; when
every such entry was read, **60 of the 132 the apparatus had accepted as sequences turned out not to
state an ordered procedure at all**, and each one released the operations it had been holding. Those
operations were then read for what they actually are, which is what the second and third classes
record. A placement that was never a procedure is not a weaker placement; it is not one.

A row placed in a sequence is named by a sequence entry — one of the zones above, a numbered
procedure run, or a finding from the whole-specification reading campaign. The specification says,
in order, what happens.

A row that is single-step has no ordered flow to belong to. Its whole procedure is the validity
rules its own section states: `SubmitNeed` produces a `NeedSubmission`, whose canonical key
derivation and `InvalidNeedSubmission` rejection are set out at §23.8. That is a complete
operation, not a type with no act. Each such row carries `evidence.defining_section` naming the
section that defines it, and S25 fails the run if that section is not a real heading of the
specification.

A row whose procedure this design supplies has neither. The specification uses the act — in an
allowlist, in a prohibition, in a schema, or in a sentence that sends the reader to a procedure it
never states — and no line states it. Those rows carry no `defining_section`, because a row whose
section does not define it may not claim one, and each is governed by a rule at 4.2 that S31 holds
it to.

The distinction between the three is the whole of this section, and two earlier forms of it got
the distinction wrong in the same way: they treated a count as a finding. The first listed 32
UNSEQUENCED rows and called the number "not a defect in itself", which conflated a row whose
sequence had not been found yet with a row that has no sequence to find. The second showed that 16
of those 32 were named by sequence entries all along — true, and it turned out to be the opposite
defect: **the entries naming them were not sequences.** They were invariant lists, prohibition
lists, property definitions, schemas and computations that had been carved into nominal steps, and
the operations had been bound to them because their names or their result objects appeared in the
lines. Reading all 430 sections separated the three classes; counting never would have.

### 2.1 Disposition

- **active** — the operation satisfies the predicate at 14029. All twenty fields are required.
- **framework** — part of the Core Operation machinery itself: the TimeHandshake stages,
  session revalidation, poll, watch, cancel, object get, proof verify, capabilities. All
  twenty fields are required, and the row is exempt from the object-coverage check because it
  produces no Gaia state object.
- **excluded** — the specification gives the name to something that does not satisfy the
  predicate, or gives it to something else entirely. Recorded with its reason so that a correct
  exclusion can never be mistaken for an omission. Sixty-nine rows carry this disposition, in
  three kinds.

  Ten are **names that are not acts**: the read-only allowlist entries (`ReadStoredObjects`,
  `VerifyObjects`, `InspectTransferState`, `InspectTemporalHealth`,
  `ReadBankDataRequiredForRecovery`, `FetchRecoveryMaterial`), the two interface frames
  (`CAPABILITIES_GET`, `OPERATION_CANCEL`), the commitment that represents a validator set
  rather than acting on one (`CommitCivicValidatorAvailability`), the status value
  `TransitionCivicNeedAssetStatus`, and `TIME_HANDSHAKE_RESPONSE`, which line 14177 does not
  list among the registry rows because it is a stage of the begin exchange rather than an
  operation.

  Thirty-five are **object types that no operation produces**, each recorded with the reason it
  has no producer: externally produced by a provider or a limited central service
  (`SoulRecord`, `StorageReceiptV2`, `TransferDistinctnessCredential`, `ExecutableMarketingPlan`
  and others), derived or computed rather than created (`AssetAccessGrant`, `AssetScore`,
  `ForumMembershipEkyc`, `CiphertextChunk` and others), baked into the binary
  (`PaymentAuthoritySet`), or a structured field rather than an independent object
  (`TimeHandshakeResponse`, `GaiaTransportEndpoint`). Their `kind` is prefixed `@object:` to make
  plain that the row accounts for a type rather than naming an operation.

  Twenty-four are **names the registry keeps but does not count**. Some are aliases for an
  operation that already has a row — `RunStorageGarbageCollection` is one act with
  `ExecuteStorageGarbageCollection` under two names. The rest were tested by the reading
  campaigns and are not distinct operations: schema headings, sub-steps of a larger procedure,
  superseded names, and interface entry points such as the generic dispatcher at 14157.

  Without the middle group the object-coverage check reports thirty-five types with no producer.
  With them the check distinguishes "nobody produces this and here is why" from "nobody produces
  this and nobody noticed", which is the distinction the check exists to draw.

### 2.2 Reject code families

Line 14173 names the field `reject_code_families`, and §22.2 supplies the families without
labelling them as such: it presents 449 codes in thirteen fenced blocks, each introduced by a
sentence naming what is validated. Those sentences are the family names. The partition is
exact — every one of the 449 codes falls in exactly one family and none is orphaned.

| family | specification lines | codes |
|---|---|---|
| `core_authority_time_soul_ekyc_payment` | 9881..10020 | 135 |
| `transfer` | 10026..10070 | 45 |
| `lineage_royalty` | 10076..10102 | 27 |
| `discovery_storage_encryption` | 10108..10171 | 61 |
| `marketing` | 10177..10209 | 33 |
| `publication` | 10215..10245 | 31 |
| `publication_units` | 10253..10262 | 10 |
| `core_operation_framework` | 10268..10308 | 41 |
| `time_witness` | 10314..10318 | 5 |
| `civic` | 10324..10346 | 23 |
| `cross_forum_policy` | 10352..10361 | 10 |
| `forum_ekyc` | 10367..10380 | 14 |
| `transport_seam` | 10386..10403 | 14 |

Those are validity rules, pre-state conditions and rejections, which is this operation's whole procedure. The acts that follow a successful challenge — the committee's BFT quorum fixing finality at 12222 and the allocation and bundle changes at 12224-12231 — belong to NeedSplitFinalization, a different object of the same section, and are consequences the finality carries rather than steps the challenger performs. Those are validity rules and rejections for one computation, not an ordered flow. §18.22.1 does state an ordered procedure, but it is the Payment-Service's atomic finalization of an ALR sale — eight numbered steps beginning with resolving the ServiceOrder and the lineage node — and creating the settlement is its step 4, not the whole of that procedure and not a second actor's act inside this one.","grounding":{"date":"2026-10-08","classification":"definition","line":12629,"note":"read on 2026-10-08, after the entry that named this operation was found not to state an ordered procedure."},"defining_section":"section 23.22 (Asset Lineage Royaltyの受理述語)"},"position":"unsequenced"}

It is one act with a contract rather than an ordered procedure: the section names no second actor and no step order, and the design's own section-level adjudication of §2.5 records exactly that ('It contrasts the two admissible forms of forum creation (parentless and parented) without prescribing an ordered procedure'); §2.6 is likewise adjudicated an invariant list whose 'creation procedure itself lives elsewhere'. the surrounding section fixes the ledger key and leaf the creation updates (5430-5446) and the monotonicity invariant credited_high_water_next >= credited_high_water_previous >= 0 (5450-5452); and 5456 states the rejection — 「credit済み高水位の低下、同一payloadでの重複credit、またはlotとledger更新の不一致は`InvalidSeedCreditLedger`として拒否する」. It is one act rather than an ordered procedure: the section names the lot's creation and the ledger update it performs as one event and orders no steps by named actors — its section-level adjudication records that §13.7.1 is 'a data schema with constraints'. The neighbouring §7.9.1 defines the lot's own field binding and arithmetic (1843: the lot uniquely binds creator, parent forum, parent payload hash, checkpoint time, the pre- and post-ledger roots, the per-lineage credit deltas, NewCreditableRealValue, RealbaseFactor and RealbaseIncrement, and 「`NewCreditableRealValue=0`の場合、origin lotを作成してはならない」, the prohibition half of the same contract), and §8.1.1's five-step run is the 親ありforumのSeedAllocation procedure — the presentation, verification and finalization of an allocation, carried live by CreateSeedAllocation (A-3902) — not the creation of the lot it consumes.","grounding":{"date":"2026-10-08","classification":"definition","line":5454,"note":"read on 2026-10-08, after the entry that named this operation was found not to state an ordered procedure."},"defining_section":"section 13.7.1 (SeedCreditLedger)"},"position":"unsequenced"}

conservation governed by §18.20 決済・分配・期限失効の全体不変条件 (line 8952)","cancellation_rule":"cancellable before the first external action starts, per the OperationStatus map; after that the map routes through failed or expired rather than cancelled","reversal_or_recovery_rule":"reversal and recovery follow the OperationStatus map through disputed, reversed, recovery_pending and recovered","rest":{"method":"POST","path":"/v1/operations/{operation-kind}"},"websocket":"operation.submit","cli":"gaia operation submit","reject_code_families":["core_authority_time_soul_ekyc_payment"],"disposition":"active","evidence":{"spec_lines":[9157],"note":"§18.22.3 states the act at 9157: 「Payment-Serviceは、各payout epochの終了時または定期バッチ時に、次のkeyごとにclaimable entitlementを集約できる。」 The section then fixes the operation's whole procedure as rules: the aggregation key (9160, network_id + beneficiary_soul_id + currency + payout_epoch_id); the split the act must perform when a key's claimable count exceeds MAX_PAYOUT_ENTITLEMENTS_PER_AGGREGATION and its six correctness conditions (9163-9170 — the part count equals ceil(count/max), each part holds 1..max, every claimable entitlement falls in exactly one part with no omission and no duplication, the order depends on canonical byte order alone, the part's amounts are computed over that part's entitlements only, and no new schema field is added); the pre-state — the six conditions an entitlement must satisfy to be an aggregation target (9174-9181); the field formulas (9183); the carry-forward rule (9185); and the rejection at 9172: an implementation that does not split, or drops entitlements from the aggregation, 「は、`ResourceLimitExceeded`として拒否する」. It is one act with a contract rather than an ordered procedure: the numbered list at 9176-9181 is a list of predicates on entitlement state, not acts, and the adjudication of A-9157 records that the span 'names no ordered acts by an actor'. The ordered run nearby is a different operation's: §18.22.1's atomic list (9129ff) resolves the sale, walks the lineage to origin, verifies policies and settles, and the live manifest carries it by FinalizeLineageRoyaltySettlement, IssuePayoutEntitlement and IssueContentAccessGrant (A-9129); aggregation happens per payout epoch, after entitlements exist.","grounding":{"date":"2026-10-08","classification":"definition","line":9157,"note":"read on 2026-10-08, after the entry that named this operation was found not to state an ordered procedure."},"defining_section":"section 18.22.3 (PayoutAggregationとpayout epoch集約)"},"position":"unsequenced"}

It changes no protocol state and produces no result object: obtaining the material is not the reinstatement, and an object signed while unhealthy is still refused without a valid lease (6054). It is one act, not an ordered procedure: the acts that produce its inputs — the designated proposer signing the CivicEpochBlock (12367) and the validators issuing prevotes and precommits (12373, 12377-12379) — are separate operations with their own rows, and §23.16 states no order among them, only the acceptance rule for the certificate. It is one act, not an ordered procedure: the proposal's generation by the submitter's local AI and its P2P distribution to the committee (12062, 12089) belong to the proposal operation, and the validators' attestations are their own operation; §23.12 states only the effect of a valid finalization and the invariants it must leave behind.","grounding":{"date":"2026-10-08","classification":"definition","line":12110,"note":"read on 2026-10-08, after the entry that named this operation was found not to state an ordered procedure."},"defining_section":"section 23.12 (NeedMergeFinalization と source need の supersession)"},"position":"unsequenced"}

after that the map routes through failed or expired rather than cancelled","reversal_or_recovery_rule":"reversal and recovery follow the OperationStatus map through disputed, reversed, recovery_pending and recovered","rest":{"method":"POST","path":"/v1/operations/{operation-kind}"},"websocket":"operation.submit","cli":"gaia operation submit","reject_code_families":["transfer"],"disposition":"active","evidence":{"spec_lines":[1328],"note":"§5.6 defines the objects of the Forum Root Succession, and 1328 is the line that states this act — '`ForumRootTransferFreeze`: 対象forumのroot authorityを書込み凍結し、root entry、checkpoint等の競合操作を停止する。freeze中は`CanIssueRootEntry`を満たさず、root ledger更新を拒否する。' — and in the same line it carries the operation's own validity rule and its rejection: for as long as the freeze is in force CanIssueRootEntry is not satisfied and a root ledger update is refused, which is what makes the freeze a state rather than a message. The section's other bullets define the agreement (1327), the succession (1329) and the dispute and resolution (1330) objects, and 1325 states the gates that must all hold before finalization, so what §5.6 sets out for this act is its object, its effect and its rule, not an order of acts. §24.10, which fixes the atomic succession, lists ForumRootTransferFreeze as a PRE-STATE (12925) and its numbered acts (12934-12938) revoke the predecessor's future root write authority and activate the successor at root_epoch + 1 — that is the finalization, a different operation, whose pre-state this freeze supplies.","grounding":{"date":"2026-10-08","classification":"definition","line":1328,"note":"read on 2026-10-08, after the entry that named this operation was found not to state an ordered procedure."},"defining_section":"section 5.6 (Forum Root Succession)"},"position":"unsequenced"}
```jsonl
{"kind":"FreezeSoulTransfer","purpose":"the freeze soul transfer procedure","mutates_protocol_state":true,"required_authority":"soul_authority_and_lease","requires_remote_time_handshake":true,"requires_session_binding":true,"required_pre_state_checkpoint":true,"required_proof_classes":[],"idempotency_scope":"FreezeSoulTransfer identity tuple: operation_id plus the canonical payload commitment","initial_status":"submitted","allowed_status_transitions":{"submitted":["accepted","rejected","cancelled","expired"],"accepted":["pending_proof","pending_finality","finalized","rejected","failed"],"pending_proof":["accepted","rejected","failed"],"pending_finality":["finalized","rejected","expired"],"finalized":[],"rejected":[],"cancelled":[],"expired":[],"failed":[]},"external_dependency_class":"none","result_object_classes":["SoulTransferFreeze"],"finality_predicate":"finalized when the single state transition at 14147 step 9 completes and no external dependency remains","cancellation_rule":"cancellable before the first external action starts, per the OperationStatus map; after that the map routes through failed or expired rather than cancelled","reversal_or_recovery_rule":"reversal and recovery follow the OperationStatus map through disputed, reversed, recovery_pending and recovered","rest":{"method":"POST","path":"/v1/operations/{operation-kind}"},"websocket":"operation.submit","cli":"gaia operation submit","reject_code_families":["transfer"],"disposition":"active","evidence":{"spec_lines":[6629],"note":"§16.7.2 defines the freeze. Line 6629 states the act — '`SoulTransferFreeze`は、対象Soulを譲渡専用の凍結状態へ置き、評価checkpoint、凍結理由、許可された限定操作、payment reservation参照を固定する不変objectである。' — and the section then fixes that act's object, its pre-state and its validity rules: the canonical fields including transfer_id, transfer_sequence, agreement_ref, source_checkpoint_refs_sorted, frozen_at, previous_soul_record_hash and resulting_soul_record_hash (6631-6644); the TransferSafeOperation allowlist permitted while frozen (6646-6662); and the operations that must not be included (6664-6680). Placing the Soul in the transfer-only frozen state and fixing those fields is one act, and the section states no sequence of acts for it. The neighbouring §16.7.1 and §16.7.3-§16.7.6 state the agreement, the payment reservation, the finalization and the dispute, and §24.9 lists SoulTransferFreeze as a pre-state of the finalization (12874) beside the valid agreement, the reserved payment and the unresolved-dispute test — those are other operations, and the freeze supplies one of their pre-states rather than being a step of them.","grounding":{"date":"2026-10-08","classification":"definition","line":6629,"note":"read on 2026-10-08, after the entry that named this operation was found not to state an ordered procedure."},"defining_section":"section 16.7.2 (SoulTransferFreeze)"},"position":"unsequenced"}

The object side is fixed at §23.18, where CivicNeedAsset.status is derived deterministically and fulfilled means the need entry has transitioned to fulfilled (12508-12533). It is one act, not an ordered procedure: the section states the condition and the effects of the transition, while the acts that precede it — NeedSubmission, NeedMergeFinalization, FIFO retirement — are other operations, each named in the same bullet list as establishing a different transition.","grounding":{"date":"2026-10-08","classification":"definition","line":12267,"note":"read on 2026-10-08, after the entry that named this operation was found not to state an ordered procedure."},"defining_section":"section 23.14 (Need lifecycle と FIFO retirement)"},"position":"unsequenced"}

The section around that line is the operation's whole procedure. 8682 fixes who may exercise it and over what — the Owner threshold alone. 8686-8708 fixes the object and its pre-state inputs: the five reason_code values, starts_at/expires_at, affected_operations, owner_set_commitment and owner_threshold_signatures. 8710-8712 fix what the act may not do, which is its rejection surface: it is not authority to change rates, reduce principal, confiscate or shorten an existing Entitlement, and it does not retroactively invalidate already verified forum state or certificates. The neighbouring §18.17 (Ownerが実行する完全手続) does state an ordered procedure, but its steps are the Owner threshold's pre-sale obligations — PaymentServiceAuthorization, the fee schedule, the asset access policy — and issuing a suspension is not among them; 8764 states outright that the suspension's procedure is 第18.16節 itself, so that sequence is not this operation's.","grounding":{"date":"2026-10-08","classification":"definition","line":8684,"note":"read on 2026-10-08, after the entry that named this operation was found not to state an ordered procedure."},"defining_section":"section 18.16 (鍵の分離と緊急停止)"},"position":"unsequenced"}

{"kind":"NormalizeLogicalOperationIntent","purpose":"the normalize logical operation intent procedure","mutates_protocol_state":true,"required_authority":"soul_authority_and_lease","requires_remote_time_handshake":true,"requires_session_binding":true,"required_pre_state_checkpoint":true,"required_proof_classes":[],"idempotency_scope":"NormalizeLogicalOperationIntent identity tuple: operation_id plus the canonical payload commitment","initial_status":"submitted","allowed_status_transitions":{"submitted":["accepted","rejected","cancelled","expired"],"accepted":["pending_proof","pending_finality","finalized","rejected","failed"],"pending_proof":["accepted","rejected","failed"],"pending_finality":["finalized","rejected","expired"],"finalized":[],"rejected":[],"cancelled":[],"expired":[],"failed":[]},"external_dependency_class":"none","result_object_classes":["LogicalOperationIntent","CoreOperationReceipt"],"finality_predicate":"finalized when the single state transition at 14147 step 9 completes and no external dependency remains","cancellation_rule":"cancellable before the first external action starts, per the OperationStatus map; after that the map routes through failed or expired rather than cancelled","reversal_or_recovery_rule":"reversal and recovery follow the OperationStatus map through disputed, reversed, recovery_pending and recovered","rest":{"method":"POST","path":"/v1/operations/{operation-kind}"},"websocket":"operation.submit","cli":"gaia operation submit","reject_code_families":["core_authority_time_soul_ekyc_payment"],"disposition":"active","evidence":{"spec_lines":[15920],"note":"§29.8 states the act at 15920 (logical intentの正規化を次に定める) and at 15918: when a request is re-signed in a new session, gaia-core normalizes it onto the same logical intent, without the caller editing the old request signature's fields in place. The section around those lines is the operation's whole procedure. 15923-15943 fixes the canonical projection of CoreOperationRequest field by field; 15945-15947 fixes logical_intent_commitment; 15950 fixes the two fields excluded from the projection (session_binding_ref and the request's current signature), forbids making any other field updatable, and forbids extending an expired logical operation by re-signing alone. 15918 and 15954 give the rejection rule — a material payload change under the same operation_id is rejected as an existing idempotency conflict, even when the body is valid for the current session — and 15952 puts logical_intent_commitment into the new receipt's signed fields. 14095 sends the reader here for the definition. It is one act by one actor, gaia-core; the requester's re-signing is the input, not a second act the section orders. The ordered procedures nearby are the Remote Session Establishment Procedure (14117-14126) and §29.3 (15849-15864), which order the handshake steps; normalization is not a handshake step — it maps a request onto a logical operation that already exists, after the session is established — so it is not an entry of theirs.","grounding":{"date":"2026-10-08","classification":"definition","line":15920,"note":"read on 2026-10-08, after the entry that named this operation was found not to state an ordered procedure."},"defining_section":"section 29.8 (不確実な結果とエラー境界)"},"position":"unsequenced"}

The section around that line is the operation's whole procedure. 6505-6517 fixes the request object, LostBodySuccessionRequest, carrying recovery_eligibility_credential_ref, recovery_epoch, request_time_interval, recovery_commitment and requester_signature. 6522-6527 fixes the pre-state conditions and validity rules: a RecoveryEligibilityCredential from a valid Owner-authorized eKYC issuer, the new body meeting health revalidation, the Soul not being in succession_contested, the prescribed cooldown and dispute window elapsed, the recovery policy threshold met, and no competing successor for the same lost incarnation. 6529 gives the rule for the contested case, which is the rejection: neither request may become active, the Soul moves to succession_contested, and ordinary authority is zero until it is cleared. {"kind":"ProposeCivicEpochBlock","purpose":"the propose civic epoch block procedure","mutates_protocol_state":true,"required_authority":"validator_quorum","requires_remote_time_handshake":true,"requires_session_binding":true,"required_pre_state_checkpoint":true,"required_proof_classes":[],"idempotency_scope":"ProposeCivicEpochBlock identity tuple: operation_id plus the canonical payload commitment","initial_status":"submitted","allowed_status_transitions":{"submitted":["accepted","rejected","cancelled","expired"],"accepted":["pending_external","finalized","rejected","failed"],"pending_external":["finalized","failed","cancelled","expired"],"finalized":[],"rejected":[],"cancelled":[],"expired":[],"failed":[]},"external_dependency_class":"civic_validator_committee","result_object_classes":["CivicEpochBlock"],"finality_predicate":"finalized only when every civic_validator_committee action reaches a terminal state; conservation governed by the section named for this family","cancellation_rule":"cancellable before the first external action starts, per the OperationStatus map; after that the map routes through failed or expired rather than cancelled","reversal_or_recovery_rule":"reversal and recovery follow the OperationStatus map through disputed, reversed, recovery_pending and recovered","rest":{"method":"POST","path":"/v1/operations/{operation-kind}"},"websocket":"operation.submit","cli":"gaia operation submit","reject_code_families":["civic"],"disposition":"active","evidence":{"spec_lines":[12367],"note":"§23.16 states the act at 12367: the CivicEpochBlock's signature is by the authority key of the validator that proposes it, and that proposer is fixed deterministically as the (state_sequence(q_e) mod n(F,e))-th validator of CivicValidatorSet(F,e) in canonical validator_soul_id order, so no forum root, eKYC provider, Payment-Service or arbitrary participant may choose it. The section around that line is the proposal's whole procedure: 12367-12368 fix the pre-state conditions (the proposer's authority key must be bound to the Soul's active DeviceIncarnation and inside a valid SoulEpochLease), 12369 fixes the chaining rule (strict monotonic increase of the snapshot checkpoint's state_sequence) together with its rejection (CivicFinalityConflict when two blocks with different descriptors or snapshot_refs gain finality for one vote_epoch_id), and 12371 fixes the prohibition (no forum root, eKYC provider or Payment-Service may sign, reject or cancel the block). It states the act at 1330: \"`ForumRootTransferDispute` / `ForumRootTransferResolution`: forum root authority譲渡に関する異議・解決を記録する。取消は履歴削除でなく解決objectにより状態を進める。\" The resolution records the objection and the resolution of a forum root authority transfer, and a cancellation advances state through a resolution object rather than deleting history. The section also sets the operation's own pre-state and bounds: 1325 requires the transfer's eKYC, distinctness, freeze, payment reservation, old-authority revocation, new-authority binding, root epoch switch, dispute state and history commitment before finalization; 1327 bounds the transferred scope to future root operating authority (ForbiddenTransferTrustCarryField); 1332 fixes the §18.21 payment path and states that no chargeback-driven automatic rollback follows finalization. It is one act — an authorized resolution that moves the dispute state — not an ordered procedure. The neighbouring ordered procedures are not this operation's: §24.10's five-step atomic forum root succession (12931-12939) is the succession's own atomic transition, and §16.7.6 (異議・取消・解決, 6789-6794), whose structure 1325 says this section shares, states bounds on a Soul Transfer resolution (no rollback on a chargeback alone; a post-finalization reversal is a new transfer) rather than an ordered flow producing this object.","grounding":{"date":"2026-10-08","classification":"definition","line":1330,"note":"read on 2026-10-08, after the entry that named this operation was found not to state an ordered procedure."},"defining_section":"section 5.6 (Forum Root Succession)"},"position":"unsequenced"}

{"kind":"RetireCivicNeedByFifo","purpose":"the retire civic need by fifo procedure","mutates_protocol_state":true,"required_authority":"validator_quorum","requires_remote_time_handshake":true,"requires_session_binding":true,"required_pre_state_checkpoint":true,"required_proof_classes":["civic_need_asset_status"],"idempotency_scope":"RetireCivicNeedByFifo identity tuple: operation_id plus the canonical payload commitment","initial_status":"submitted","allowed_status_transitions":{"submitted":["accepted","rejected","cancelled","expired"],"accepted":["pending_external","finalized","rejected","failed"],"pending_external":["finalized","failed","cancelled","expired"],"finalized":[],"rejected":[],"cancelled":[],"expired":[],"failed":[]},"external_dependency_class":"civic_validator_committee","result_object_classes":["CivicNeedAsset"],"finality_predicate":"finalized only when every civic_validator_committee action reaches a terminal state; conservation governed by the section named for this family","cancellation_rule":"cancellable before the first external action starts, per the OperationStatus map; after that the map routes through failed or expired rather than cancelled","reversal_or_recovery_rule":"reversal and recovery follow the OperationStatus map through disputed, reversed, recovery_pending and recovered","rest":{"method":"POST","path":"/v1/operations/{operation-kind}"},"websocket":"operation.submit","cli":"gaia operation submit","reject_code_families":["civic"],"disposition":"active","evidence":{"spec_lines":[12024],"note":"Section 23.9 states the act at 12024: \"新しいactive need又はmerged needの追加後にbundle件数が`K(F)`を超える場合、最古のactive entryをFIFOにより退場させる。\" After a new or merged active need is added, if the bundle count would exceed K(F), the oldest active entry is retired. The same section sets the operation's own procedure, pre-state and rejection codes: the loop that runs the retirement until the capacity condition holds (12027-12029: while ActiveCivicNeedCount(F, t) > K(F): retire oldest active CivicNeedBundleEntry), the deterministic FIFO order that decides which entry is oldest (11998-12002 and the sort_by of 12031: accepted_sequence ascending, then need_id canonical byte order), the binding of accepted_sequence to the state_sequence of the finalizing checkpoint (12006-12012), and the rejection codes at 12031: CivicNeedBundleCapacityExceeded, CivicNeedFifoSequenceReset (rewriting a finalized accepted_sequence or re-issuing a smaller one) and CivicNeedFifoOrderViolation (changing the order by CivicNeedScore or LLM output). It is one act repeated until the capacity condition holds, decided by a fixed order, not an ordered procedure by named actors. §23.14 records the lifecycle transition the act produces (12265-12294: active -> retired_by_fifo, and what a retired need may not do — it leaves the bundle, is not counted, targets no active CivicNeedScore, and its allocations become inactive) but states no procedure of its own.","grounding":{"date":"2026-10-08","classification":"definition","line":12024,"note":"read on 2026-10-08, after the entry that named this operation was found not to state an ordered procedure."},"defining_section":"section 23.9 (active CivicNeedBundle と FIFO)"},"position":"unsequenced"}

(2675-2716), the conditions a contributing transaction must already satisfy (2609-2622: enabled policy, in-scope kind, forum-bound order, paid receipt, valid settlement, not cancelled by refund/dispute/chargeback, the self-purchase and common-identity exclusions, the lifetime cap and the settlement_finality_delay), and the ordered state flow the record takes (2723-2729: PaymentSettlement.finalized -> pending_finality -> settlement_finality_time reached -> eligible_for_distribution -> included in a ForumRevenuePoolDistribution). There are no rejection codes to state because §22.1 line 694 makes the heat objects non-consensus: a missing, malformed, stale or unverifiable heat object must not invalidate an otherwise valid protocol object or transaction. The section states no ordered procedure — the neighbouring §12.10.8 fixes a penalty formula and §12.10.10 an audit obligation, neither of them a flow this operation is a step of — so the operation is single-step, not a step in a sequence.","grounding":{"date":"2026-10-08","classification":"definition","line":5245,"note":"read on 2026-10-08, after the entry that named this operation was found not to state an ordered procedure."},"defining_section":"section 12.10.9 (Kill switches, rollback, and configuration validity)"},"position":"unsequenced"}

{"kind":"SubmitCivicPrecommit","purpose":"the submit civic precommit procedure","mutates_protocol_state":true,"required_authority":"validator_quorum","requires_remote_time_handshake":true,"requires_session_binding":true,"required_pre_state_checkpoint":true,"required_proof_classes":["civic_vote_tally_validity"],"idempotency_scope":"SubmitCivicPrecommit identity tuple: operation_id plus the canonical payload commitment","initial_status":"submitted","allowed_status_transitions":{"submitted":["accepted","rejected","cancelled","expired"],"accepted":["pending_external","finalized","rejected","failed"],"pending_external":["finalized","failed","cancelled","expired"],"finalized":[],"rejected":[],"cancelled":[],"expired":[],"failed":[]},"external_dependency_class":"civic_validator_committee","result_object_classes":["CivicPrecommit"],"finality_predicate":"finalized only when every civic_validator_committee action reaches a terminal state; conservation governed by the section named for this family","cancellation_rule":"cancellable before the first external action starts, per the OperationStatus map; after that the map routes through failed or expired rather than cancelled","reversal_or_recovery_rule":"reversal and recovery follow the OperationStatus map through disputed, reversed, recovery_pending and recovered","rest":{"method":"POST","path":"/v1/operations/{operation-kind}"},"websocket":"operation.submit","cli":"gaia operation submit","reject_code_families":["civic"],"disposition":"active","evidence":{"spec_lines":[12357],"note":"§23.16 fixes the act and the operation's own rules in one place. Line 12373 states it: '投票対象は`epoch_block_ref`が指す単一の`CivicEpochBlock`である。決定語彙は`accept`及び`commit`の2値とし、`CivicPrevote`が`accept`、`CivicPrecommit`が`commit`を表す。`reject`はobject型として表現せず、当該validatorが対応するvoteを発行しないことが`reject`の唯一の表現である。' The section then gives the pre-conditions and the rejections: each vote is uniquely identified by (forum_id, vote_epoch_id, epoch_block_ref, validator_soul_id) and the validator's inclusion must be proved against validator_set_root; a bad signature or authority binding, a mismatched forum_id or vote_epoch_id, an unresolvable epoch_block_ref or a validator outside CivicValidatorSet(F,e) is rejected as CivicPrecommitInvalid; and equivocation on a different epoch_block_ref prevents finality for the epoch (CivicFinalityConflict). That is one act performed by one validator — casting a single commit precommit on the epoch block — and the section states no order among the votes, so it is single-step. The part of §23.16 that does describe an ordered flow (block proposal, prevote, precommit, CivicFinalityCertificate) belongs to the finality machinery: it counts precommits as quorum inputs rather than sequencing this submission, and the precommit is what feeds it rather than a step of it.","grounding":{"date":"2026-10-08","classification":"definition","line":12373,"note":"read on 2026-10-08, after the entry that named this operation was found not to state an ordered procedure."},"defining_section":"section 23.16 (CivicEpochBlock、Prevote、Precommit、FinalityCertificate)"},"position":"unsequenced"}
{"kind":"SubmitCivicPrevote","purpose":"the submit civic prevote procedure","mutates_protocol_state":true,"required_authority":"validator_quorum","requires_remote_time_handshake":true,"requires_session_binding":true,"required_pre_state_checkpoint":true,"required_proof_classes":["civic_vote_tally_validity"],"idempotency_scope":"SubmitCivicPrevote identity tuple: operation_id plus the canonical payload commitment","initial_status":"submitted","allowed_status_transitions":{"submitted":["accepted","rejected","cancelled","expired"],"accepted":["pending_external","finalized","rejected","failed"],"pending_external":["finalized","failed","cancelled","expired"],"finalized":[],"rejected":[],"cancelled":[],"expired":[],"failed":[]},"external_dependency_class":"civic_validator_committee","result_object_classes":["CivicPrevote"],"finality_predicate":"finalized only when every civic_validator_committee action reaches a terminal state; conservation governed by the section named for this family","cancellation_rule":"cancellable before the first external action starts, per the OperationStatus map; after that the map routes through failed or expired rather than cancelled","reversal_or_recovery_rule":"reversal and recovery follow the OperationStatus map through disputed, reversed, recovery_pending and recovered","rest":{"method":"POST","path":"/v1/operations/{operation-kind}"},"websocket":"operation.submit","cli":"gaia operation submit","reject_code_families":["civic"],"disposition":"active","evidence":{"spec_lines":[12373],"note":"§23.16 states the act and the operation's own rules together. Line 12373 states it: '`CivicPrevote`及び`CivicPrecommit`の投票対象の表現と決定語彙を次で固定する。投票対象は`epoch_block_ref`が指す単一の`CivicEpochBlock`である。決定語彙は`accept`及び`commit`の2値とし、`CivicPrevote`が`accept`、`CivicPrecommit`が`commit`を表す。' The section then gives the pre-conditions and the rejections: the vote is identified by (forum_id, vote_epoch_id, epoch_block_ref, validator_soul_id), the validator must be in CivicValidatorSet(F,e) with its inclusion proved against validator_set_root, duplicate votes by one validator on one block count once, and a bad signature, bad authority binding, mismatched forum_id or vote_epoch_id, unresolvable epoch_block_ref or validator outside the set is rejected as CivicPrevoteInvalid. That is one act by one validator — casting a single accept prevote on the epoch block — and the section states no order among the votes, so it is single-step. The quorum rule that does read like a sequence ('quorumは、`accept`のprevoteが`2f + 1`以上、かつ`commit`のprecommitが`2f + 1`以上であることを要求する') is a counting condition on the collected votes, not this operation's place in a flow; the ordered flow in §23.16 is the epoch block and its finality certificate, which consume the prevote rather than sequence it.","grounding":{"date":"2026-10-08","classification":"definition","line":12373,"note":"read on 2026-10-08, after the entry that named this operation was found not to state an ordered procedure."},"defining_section":"section 23.16 (CivicEpochBlock、Prevote、Precommit、FinalityCertificate)"},"position":"unsequenced"}

after that the map routes through failed or expired rather than cancelled","reversal_or_recovery_rule":"reversal and recovery follow the OperationStatus map through disputed, reversed, recovery_pending and recovered","rest":{"method":"POST","path":"/v1/operations/{operation-kind}"},"websocket":"operation.submit","cli":"gaia operation submit","reject_code_families":["marketing"],"disposition":"active","evidence":{"spec_lines":[5170],"note":"§12.10.6 states the act — the engine moves a forum's Heat Stabilizer state from prior_state to next_state — through the per-transition requirements at 5192-5197: normal -> watch requires WatchCondition; watch -> warm requires WarmCondition; warm -> hot requires HotCondition for at least hot_enter_epochs consecutive finalized evaluation epochs; hot -> recovering occurs when HotCondition is false for at least one finalized evaluation epoch; recovering -> normal requires NOT WatchCondition for hot_exit_epochs consecutive finalized evaluation epochs; watch -> normal and warm -> normal occur when NOT WatchCondition holds for the configured cooling duration. The section is the operation's whole procedure: the pre-state is fixed by the prior transition (5178 makes the machine deterministic for the same HeatObservation, HeatStabilizerPolicy and prior HeatStateTransition), the freshness bounds hot_enter_epochs >= 1 and hot_exit_epochs >= hot_enter_epochs are the validity rules (5200), and 5198 states the rejection — any missing, stale, malformed, non-finalized or unverifiable required input produces observation_unavailable and MUST NOT produce a new hot transition. It is ONE act by one actor (the engine, 5251) and not an ordered procedure: the conditions are a rule table over a single state variable, and the only other acts §12.10 names govern different objects — production of the HeatObservation and emission of the HeatInterventionDecision (5251) and the HeatStabilizerConfiguration of §12.10.9, whose schemas §22.7 fixes without stating an act.","grounding":{"date":"2026-10-08","classification":"definition","line":5192,"note":"read on 2026-10-08, after the entry that named this operation was found not to state an ordered procedure."},"defining_section":"section 12.10.6 (State machine and hysteresis)"},"position":"unsequenced"}

{"kind":"ContestSuccession","purpose":"the contest succession procedure","mutates_protocol_state":true,"required_authority":"soul_authority_and_lease","requires_remote_time_handshake":true,"requires_session_binding":true,"required_pre_state_checkpoint":true,"required_proof_classes":[],"idempotency_scope":"ContestSuccession identity tuple: operation_id plus the canonical payload commitment","initial_status":"submitted","allowed_status_transitions":{"submitted":["accepted","rejected","cancelled","expired"],"accepted":["pending_external","finalized","rejected","failed"],"pending_external":["finalized","failed","cancelled","expired"],"finalized":[],"rejected":[],"cancelled":[],"expired":[],"failed":[]},"external_dependency_class":"none","result_object_classes":["SoulTransferDispute"],"finality_predicate":"finalized when the single state transition at 14147 step 9 completes","cancellation_rule":"cancellable before the first external action starts, per the OperationStatus map","reversal_or_recovery_rule":"reversal and recovery follow the OperationStatus map through disputed, reversed, recovery_pending and recovered","rest":{"method":"POST","path":"/v1/operations/{operation-kind}"},"websocket":"operation.submit","cli":"gaia operation submit","reject_code_families":["transfer"],"disposition":"active","position":"unsequenced","evidence":{"spec_lines":[6529],"note":"Section 16.5 states the act at 6529: 「競合するsuccession requestがある場合、Soul-Bankはどちらもactiveにしてはならない。Soulを`succession_contested`に遷移させ、解除まで通常authorityは0とする。」 When more than one succession request names the same lost incarnation the Soul is moved to succession_contested and normal authority is zero until the contest is released. The same section fixes the operation's own pre-state conditions at 6520-6527 (a valid Owner-authorized RecoveryEligibilityCredential, successor health revalidation, a Soul not already in succession_contested, an elapsed cooldown and dispute window, the recovery-policy threshold, and no competing successor for the same lost incarnation) and its rejection at 6529, so the section is the operation's whole procedure. It is one act, not an ordered procedure: section 24.2 (12765) states only the single valid transition (active(old), pending(new)) to (retired(old), active(new)) as a state-machine invariant, and section 16.7.6 at 6789 only records that SoulTransferDispute and SoulTransferResolution are appended, presupposing the contest while stating neither its actor nor its order.","grounding":{"date":"2026-10-08","classification":"definition","line":6529,"note":"read on 2026-10-08, after the entry that named this operation was found not to state an ordered procedure."},"defining_section":"section 16.5 (喪失転生)"}}

{"kind":"QuarantineForkedIncarnation","purpose":"the quarantine forked incarnation procedure","mutates_protocol_state":true,"required_authority":"soul_authority_and_lease","requires_remote_time_handshake":true,"requires_session_binding":true,"required_pre_state_checkpoint":true,"required_proof_classes":[],"idempotency_scope":"QuarantineForkedIncarnation identity tuple: operation_id plus the canonical payload commitment","initial_status":"submitted","allowed_status_transitions":{"submitted":["accepted","rejected","cancelled","expired"],"accepted":["pending_external","finalized","rejected","failed"],"pending_external":["finalized","failed","cancelled","expired"],"finalized":[],"rejected":[],"cancelled":[],"expired":[],"failed":[]},"external_dependency_class":"none","result_object_classes":["AuthorityForkEvidence"],"finality_predicate":"finalized when the single state transition at 14147 step 9 completes","cancellation_rule":"cancellable before the first external action starts, per the OperationStatus map","reversal_or_recovery_rule":"reversal and recovery follow the OperationStatus map through disputed, reversed, recovery_pending and recovered","rest":{"method":"POST","path":"/v1/operations/{operation-kind}"},"websocket":"operation.submit","cli":"gaia operation submit","reject_code_families":["core_authority_time_soul_ekyc_payment"],"disposition":"active","position":"unsequenced","evidence":{"spec_lines":[6292],"note":"Section 15.4 first states the detection rule at 6279 - when one active body issues different signed objects from the same operation_sequence or the same previous_operation_hash, a fork is detected - and declares the AuthorityForkEvidence object (6282-6290) that records it. The act itself is stated at 6292: 「検証できたforumは、当該incarnationを`quarantined`とする。」 with the accompanying statement that this is objective health evidence rather than crime information and that the authority operation chain can also be audited from Soul-Bank's SoulRecord and from each receiving forum. It is one act - set the incarnation to quarantined - whose pre-state condition (an observed fork on the authority operation chain) and whose result (the evidence object plus the quarantined status) are both set out in this section, so the section is its whole procedure. Section 15.5 at 6298 restates the enforcement as detection and quarantine plus invalidation of old epoch keys and names no further acts or actors, and section 13.4 at 5370 handles a different object - a fork of checkpoint payloads, rejected as EquivocatedFinality - so neither states an ordered procedure for this operation.","grounding":{"date":"2026-10-08","classification":"definition","line":6292,"note":"read on 2026-10-08, after the entry that named this operation was found not to state an ordered procedure."},"defining_section":"section 15.4 (AuthorityOperationHeaderとauthority操作列)"}}
{"kind":"SuspendForEkyc","purpose":"the suspend for ekyc procedure","mutates_protocol_state":true,"required_authority":"ekyc_provider_authorization","requires_remote_time_handshake":true,"requires_session_binding":true,"required_pre_state_checkpoint":true,"required_proof_classes":[],"idempotency_scope":"SuspendForEkyc identity tuple: operation_id plus the canonical payload commitment","initial_status":"submitted","allowed_status_transitions":{"submitted":["accepted","rejected","cancelled","expired"],"accepted":["pending_external","finalized","rejected","failed"],"pending_external":["finalized","failed","cancelled","expired"],"finalized":[],"rejected":[],"cancelled":[],"expired":[],"failed":[]},"external_dependency_class":"ekyc_provider","result_object_classes":["EkycRevocationState"],"finality_predicate":"finalized when the single state transition at 14147 step 9 completes","cancellation_rule":"cancellable before the first external action starts, per the OperationStatus map","reversal_or_recovery_rule":"reversal and recovery follow the OperationStatus map through disputed, reversed, recovery_pending and recovered","rest":{"method":"POST","path":"/v1/operations/{operation-kind}"},"websocket":"operation.submit","cli":"gaia operation submit","reject_code_families":["core_authority_time_soul_ekyc_payment"],"disposition":"active","position":"unsequenced","evidence":{"spec_lines":[7267],"note":"§17.11 is the section named by the state this operation produces, and it states the act together with the operation's own pre-state and effects. The act line is 7267: 「active -> suspended_for_ekyc」, whose pre-state 7264 fixes — a forum whose membership_ekyc_policy is verified_required, and a Soul whose eKYC credential has become 失効・撤回・検証不能, with the past root_entry or community neither erased nor rewritten. 7270-7281 enumerate what the suspended Soul may not do (active membershipの主張, 通常authority, T_actualの積算, depthに基づく通常権限, CanIssue, CanIssueTo, AssetScore as an active member, Candidates / Reach / CivicCandidates / CivicReach, forum asset access conditions, Civic validator / ballot / need participation), 7283-7290 what it may still do (stored objectの読取と検証, eKYC renewal, ForumEkycParticipationProofの生成, ForumMembershipEkycRenewalの提出, recovery / time synchronization / health revalidation, archive取得), and 7292 fixes the invariant that suspension is not a membership-history state and must not enter ActiveMembers or membership_root (第13.2節). That is one transition with its conditions and its effects, not an ordered procedure: the section names no second actor and sequences no two acts. The neighbouring §17.10 is the ordered renewal procedure on the other side of the same edge (suspended_for_ekyc -> active) and is a different operation; §17.6 defines EkycRevocationState, the object the Owner-authorized eKYC service issues and what makes a credential 検証不能, so that section defines the triggering object and not the suspension.","grounding":{"date":"2026-10-08","classification":"definition","line":7267,"note":"read on 2026-10-08, after the entry that named this operation was found not to state an ordered procedure."},"defining_section":"section 17.11 (suspended_for_ekyc)"}}

{"kind":"QuarantineAnomalousPayment","purpose":"the quarantine anomalous payment procedure","mutates_protocol_state":true,"required_authority":"payment_service_authorization","requires_remote_time_handshake":true,"requires_session_binding":true,"required_pre_state_checkpoint":true,"required_proof_classes":[],"idempotency_scope":"QuarantineAnomalousPayment identity tuple: operation_id plus the canonical payload commitment","initial_status":"submitted","allowed_status_transitions":{"submitted":["accepted","rejected","cancelled","expired"],"accepted":["pending_external","finalized","rejected","failed"],"pending_external":["finalized","failed","cancelled","expired"],"finalized":[],"rejected":[],"cancelled":[],"expired":[],"failed":[]},"external_dependency_class":"stripe_payment","result_object_classes":["PaymentReceipt"],"finality_predicate":"finalized when every external action reaches a terminal state; conservation governed by §18.20 決済・分配・期限失効の全体不変条件 (line 8952)","cancellation_rule":"cancellable before the first external action starts, per the OperationStatus map","reversal_or_recovery_rule":"reversal and recovery follow the OperationStatus map through disputed, reversed, recovery_pending and recovered","rest":{"method":"POST","path":"/v1/operations/{operation-kind}"},"websocket":"operation.submit","cli":"gaia operation submit","reject_code_families":["core_authority_time_soul_ekyc_payment"],"disposition":"active","position":"unsequenced","evidence":{"spec_lines":[2670],"note":"§7.17.4 states when a Pool contribution arises, and its last paragraph states this act together with its trigger. The gate is set out immediately above (2650-2668: the chosen global network fee plus the raw forum Pool amount must stay under net_after_stripe, and in amount form net_after_stripe_minor - gaia_network_maintenance_fee_minor - raw_forum_revenue_pool_contribution_minor >= 1), and 2670 states the act: 「この条件を満たさない注文は、決済開始前に拒否しなければならない。既にStripe決済が完了した後に検出した場合、Payment-Serviceは自動的に販売者取り分を負にしてはならず、決済異常として隔離し、Owner認可済みの例外処理・返金手続に従う。」 The sentence fixes the operation's pre-state (a completed Stripe payment whose contribution arithmetic fails the guard), its boundary (the Payment-Service must not make the seller's share negative) and its effect (the payment is quarantined as a settlement anomaly, 決済異常として隔離). That is one act with its conditions, not an ordered procedure — the section sequences no second actor's acts. The same act recurs at 8781 as item 10 of §18.18.1's list of Payment-Service duties (署名不正、未知account、metadata不一致、金額不一致、通貨不一致、重複注文、状態逆行はfail-closedで隔離する), which is a requirements enumeration rather than this operation's sequence; the refund path the sentence names is a different operation, stated at §18.13; and §18.16 defines EmergencyPaymentSuspension, the Owner threshold's halt of new_checkout, new_transfer and new_payout, a different act over a different object.","grounding":{"date":"2026-10-08","classification":"definition","line":2670,"note":"read on 2026-10-08, after the entry that named this operation was found not to state an ordered procedure."},"defining_section":"section 7.17.4 (Pool contributionの発生)"}}

{"kind":"ReviseCivicBallot","purpose":"the revise civic ballot procedure","mutates_protocol_state":true,"required_authority":"validator_quorum","requires_remote_time_handshake":true,"requires_session_binding":true,"required_pre_state_checkpoint":true,"required_proof_classes":["civic_vote_validity","civic_citizen_status"],"idempotency_scope":"ReviseCivicBallot identity tuple: operation_id plus the canonical payload commitment","initial_status":"submitted","allowed_status_transitions":{"submitted":["accepted","rejected","cancelled","expired"],"accepted":["pending_external","finalized","rejected","failed"],"pending_external":["finalized","failed","cancelled","expired"],"finalized":[],"rejected":[],"cancelled":[],"expired":[],"failed":[]},"external_dependency_class":"none","result_object_classes":["CivicBallot"],"finality_predicate":"finalized when the single state transition at 14147 step 9 completes","cancellation_rule":"cancellable before the first external action starts, per the OperationStatus map","reversal_or_recovery_rule":"reversal and recovery follow the OperationStatus map through disputed, reversed, recovery_pending and recovered","rest":{"method":"POST","path":"/v1/operations/{operation-kind}"},"websocket":"operation.submit","cli":"gaia operation submit","reject_code_families":["civic"],"disposition":"active","position":"unsequenced","evidence":{"spec_lines":[11788],"note":"§23.4 fixes the ballot's canonical shape and its validity contract, and line 11788 states this act: 「利用者はballot revisionにより、epoch内で票を再配分できる（第23.5節）。」 The section's rules are the revision's own, because a revision is a ballot. 11753 makes one current ballot per CivicCitizen per forum and epoch and 11755-11772 fix the CivicBallot and CivicBallotAllocation fields including revision_sequence and previous_ballot_ref_optional; 11774-11784 bound each allocation 0 <= allocated_votes <= V_F and the whole ballot sum <= V_F, rejected as CivicBallotVoteBudgetExceeded; 11786 requires allocations_sorted in allocation_id byte order with a unique allocation_id, requires origin_need_id = target_need_id for a ballot the voter writes himself, and names InvalidCivicBallot, InvalidCivicBallotAllocation, CivicBallotOriginTargetMismatch and CivicBallotInactiveNeedTarget; 11811 refuses a non-CivicCitizen's CivicBallot as a civic ballot and reduces it to a Wild Signal submission. The neighbour §23.5 adds the revision chain's own conditions — revision_sequence = 0 or PreviousBallot(B).revision_sequence + 1, PreviousBallotReferenceIsCorrect, CivicBallotRevisionGap, CivicBallotNullifierEquivocation and CivicBallotRevisionFork — and §23.3 fixes the epoch the ballot must name; each is a validity predicate on the same one act, and none is an ordered procedure. ## 4. What this document decides
```

Four closures are decisions rather than readings, each with its override condition:

| decision | override condition |
|---|---|
| `external_dependency_class` uses the twelve values of the checks document | the specification enumerates the field, or a package RFC declares a value the list lacks |
| `NextRequiredAction`, a type the specification uses at 14088 without defining, is not a separate field; the required next action is carried in `finality_predicate` prose | the specification defines the type, or `gaia-core`'s RFC declares its variants |
| the authority vocabulary is the thirteen values of the checks document | the specification enumerates authority classes |
| the framework kinds keep `gaia-operation`'s SCREAMING_SNAKE spelling | that RFC is revised to PascalCase, in which case this registry follows |


### 4.1 Rules this design supplies where the specification is silent

The specification does not close every situation the registry has to answer. Nineteen such gaps
survived two independent whole-specification searches, each run on the instruction to falsify the
claim that no line governs it. A gap that survives both is not a defect to leave open and it is not
a licence to invent: it is a decision the design makes, and the decision belongs in the design
rather than in the finding that surfaced it.

Each row below is one such decision. `rule` is what this design adopts, in the words the row was
decided in. `grounds` are the lines the rule rests on. `why` is why those lines support it, and
`override` is the fact that would overturn it — a decision without an override is not a decision
but a premise that cannot be revised. `affects` names what the rule governs.

The rules are prose and are read. The grounds are line numbers and are checked on every run: each
must be a line of the specification that carries text, and a rule may not rest on a fence marker
or a blank line. The checker also holds these rows against the `decision` each gap carries in the
manifest, field for field, so the deliverable cannot drift from the finding it came from.

```jsonl
{"gap":"E-freeze-health-gate-composition","affects":"which operations a Soul may exercise when it is under transfer freeze and its body is not healthy at once; the permitted set is the intersection of the two allowlists (nine operations: ReadStoredObjects, VerifyObjects, InspectTemporalHealth, SynchronizeClock, RequestTimeAttestation, RespondToTimeChallenge, RequestRevalidation, FetchRecoveryMaterial, ReceiveReinstatementProof)","rule":"For a body that is both under transfer freeze and not healthy, the permitted set is the INTERSECTION of the two allowlists: ReadStoredObjects, VerifyObjects, InspectTemporalHealth, SynchronizeClock, RequestTimeAttestation, RespondToTimeChallenge, RequestRevalidation, FetchRecoveryMaterial, ReceiveReinstatementProof (nine operations).","grounds":[6018,6646],"why":"6018 and 6648 are both stated as allowlists (\"許可されるのは次の操作だけである\"), so membership is a necessary condition, not an offer. Two necessary conditions compose by conjunction. The union reading would let a frozen unhealthy body submit a recovery proof (health-only) while frozen, or submit a transfer dispute (freeze-only) while unhealthy, breaching the other gate. 6018 also states the floor directly: an unhealthy body must advance no normal Gaia state, and adding freeze can only restrict further.","override":"If a line states that one gate supersedes the other for a frozen unhealthy body, the composition becomes that gate's list alone."}
{"gap":"E-health-gate-unplaced-in-execution-order","affects":"the position of the section 14.8 health gate inside the 14147 execution order, and the reject-code family emitted when it fires together with a gate that order does place","rule":"The section 14.8 health gate is evaluated inside step (4) of the 14147 order, jointly with the authority and lease checks, and BEFORE the transfer-freeze check within that step. When a request violates both the health gate and another step, the emitted reject belongs to the health gate family.","grounds":[14147,6018],"why":"Step (4) is the subject-state step: Soul identity, DeviceIncarnation, lease, trust epoch, transfer freeze. Health is a subject-state property of exactly that kind. It precedes the freeze check because 6018 bars every state-advancing operation for an unhealthy body while freeze bars only a named family, and a bar on everything must be evaluated before a bar on a subset or the reject code would name the narrower cause.","override":"If a line places the health gate in a different step, or names a reject code for the combined case."}
{"gap":"E-joint-385","affects":"the founding act that signs a child forum genesis, and which forum's health predicate that act evaluates","rule":"The forum named at 385 for the founder's health check is the PARENT forum, not the new child forum.","grounds":[383,385],"why":"The paragraph establishes parent-forum participation as the founding basis and requires an inclusion proof against the parent's membership_root at a parent checkpoint strictly before child genesis. The child forum has no members, no checkpoint and no health history at the moment of its own genesis, so no health predicate can be evaluated against it at issued_at(child_genesis).","override":"If a line states that the founder's health is evaluated against the new child forum."}
{"gap":"E-joint-796","affects":"the obligation of a StateProofEnvelope to carry inclusion and non-inclusion proofs for the optional discovery, storage and resource-contribution roots","rule":"The proof obligation at 796 is scoped to the roots belonging to the state the envelope CLAIMS. An envelope claiming transfer state carries proofs for the transfer roots and need not carry proofs for the discovery roots. For every optional root family, absence means the same as 798 states for the heat roots: no committed registry, never a negative fact.","grounds":[786,796,798],"why":"796's obligation is attached to \"譲渡状態を主張する StateProofEnvelope\", that is, to a claimed state, and a claiming envelope can only prove what it claims. 798 fixes the absence semantics for the heat roots and states the general principle: absence is unavailable state, not a negative membership, asset, payment or authority fact.","override":"If a line requires every StateProofEnvelope to carry proofs for all roots regardless of the claim."}
{"gap":"E-joint-8480","affects":"the refund path of section 18.13 for an entitlement already forfeited to network reserve or to an issued service credit","rule":"A refund requested after the entitlement has expired succeeds only against what remains in claimable_minor. The already-forfeited principal is NOT re-drawn from network reserve or from an issued service credit. The refund completes as a recovery action with the shortfall recorded as 不足額 / 回収不能額 in PayoutRecoveryAction, and step 10 settles the amounts under the conservation identity.","grounds":[8480,8482,8418],"why":"Step 6 reserves claimable_minor to reserved_minor only 返金に必要な範囲で, so the reservable amount is bounded by what is still claimable. Step 9 already provides for 不足額 and 回収不能額, which would be meaningless if the shortfall could be funded elsewhere. 8418 states a service credit does not reduce the entitlement principal and is a separate finite service obligation, so drawing on it to restore a forfeited principal would be a second claim on the same value.","override":"If a line permits network reserve or an issued credit to fund a refund of forfeited principal."}
{"gap":"E-joint-8418","affects":"the two service-credit grants for one entitlement: the mid-life payout-inability grant and the expiry grant","rule":"The two service-credit grants are not additive on the same value. A GaiaServiceCreditGrant records its entitlement, its principal and the amount already granted; a later grant for the same entitlement is computed on unclaimed_minor minus already_granted_minor for that entitlement, never on the gross unclaimed_minor.","grounds":[8415,8418],"why":"8418 states the credit does not reduce principal_minor or claimable_minor, which is exactly why the gross figure can be used twice by mistake. The conservation identity granted_units = consumed_units + expired_units + remaining_units makes the already-granted amount the only quantity that can bound a second grant without breaking conservation.","override":"If a line states the mid-life and expiry grants are independent or explicitly additive."}
{"gap":"E-joint-8484","affects":"the destination of the amount left in reserved_minor after a partial recovery of a refund","rule":"reserved_minor is consumed by the refund; it does not return to claimable. A partial recovery from the seller changes only how much of the refund is recovered, recorded as 回収額 / 不足額 / 回収不能額 in PayoutRecoveryAction. The unrecovered remainder is a loss on the refund, not a reinstatement of the seller's claimable balance.","grounds":[8480,8481,8484],"why":"Step 6 moves the amount into reserved_minor for the purpose of the refund (返金に必要な範囲で), so once the refund settles, the reservation has discharged its purpose. 8481 states that a successful Stripe refund and a successful recovery from the seller are separate facts held in separate objects, which is why the partial recovery cannot rewrite the entitlement state.","override":"If a line states that a partially recovered refund returns the unrecovered part to claimable_minor."}
{"gap":"E-joint-6661","affects":"the operations FinalizeTransferWhenAuthorized and SubmitTransferDispute, which the freeze gate permits while the body is suspended_for_ekyc","rule":"A body suspended_for_ekyc retains the TransferSafeOperation set. Suspension removes normal authority (section 17.11) and TransferSafeOperation is by definition the set that proceeds without it, so the two sets do not conflict: the removal applies to the normal-authority operations, which are outside this set.","grounds":[6646,6661],"why":"The set is named for transfer-safe progression under a transfer freeze, which is itself a state in which normal authority is withdrawn. If its members required normal authority the set would be empty in exactly the state it exists for. Where an operation appears in both the suspended-forbidden set and this set, the operation is transfer-gated rather than authority-gated, and the transfer gate governs.","override":"If a line states that suspended_for_ekyc withdraws TransferSafeOperation, or names an operation that needs normal authority in this set."}
{"gap":"E-joint-6525","affects":"the lost-body-succession cooldown and the dispute-acceptance window required by section 16.5","rule":"The lost-succession cooldown and the dispute-acceptance window are policy-carried durations on the recovery policy, not new objects. They are required fields of the policy, evaluated at the single finalized checkpoint time q as checkpoint_time(q) - declared_at >= cooldown and >= dispute_window.","grounds":[6524,6525],"why":"6524 lists them among the conditions checked against \"recovery policyのthreshold条件\", so the specification already treats them as policy thresholds. The evaluation time is fixed by the single-checkpoint rule for all forum-state-dependent values.","override":"If a line fixes numeric values for either duration, or stores them on an object other than the policy."}
{"gap":"E-joint-6895","affects":"the credential purpose forum_root_transfer and the predicate that authorizes it","rule":"The credential purpose forum_root_transfer is authorized by the same predicate shape as soul_transfer: unrevoked and unexpired at the evaluation checkpoint, bound to the transferring controller rather than the successor, carrying legal_transfer_eligibility = true, and with beneficial_owner_scope_commitment covering the forum root's beneficial owner scope.","grounds":[6889,6894,6895],"why":"6889 states that a credential used for transfer must carry transfer tier and legal-transfer eligibility. 6895's purpose set is the enumeration the predicate reads, and forum_root_transfer is a transfer purpose in it, so it takes the transfer predicate rather than needing a separate one.","override":"If a line gives forum_root_transfer a distinct predicate, or binds it to a scope other than the forum root."}
{"gap":"E-joint-6388","affects":"the joint state of transfer_lifecycle_state and lifecycle_state on one SoulRecord","rule":"The two lifecycle fields are orthogonal and need no mutual-exclusion rule. The constraint is a partial order on the pair: transfer_lifecycle_state must be none whenever lifecycle_state is retired or lost_recovery_pending, because no controller then exists to transfer from. Every other combination is permitted.","grounds":[6388,6150],"why":"6388 introduces the separate field precisely so the Soul lifecycle and an in-flight transfer are not confused, which is an argument for orthogonality rather than exclusion. The only combination the two orders forbid is a non-none transfer state without a controller.","override":"If a line states the fields are exclusive, or gives a joint transition table for the pair."}
{"gap":"E-joint-6122","affects":"the IdentityBindingCredential that binds the subject Soul after Soul Transfer finalization","rule":"After Soul Transfer finalization, the IdentityBindingCredential that binds the subject Soul is the successor controller's. The predecessor's credential is not revoked — it still identifies the predecessor as a legal subject — but it no longer binds this Soul.","grounds":[6122,6124,11739],"why":"6122 requires a post-transfer identity-key action to carry the current controller's valid eKYC, current trust epoch and active lease, so the binding follows the controller. 11739 already states that the pre-transfer controller's credential cannot serve the successor's CivicCitizen eligibility, which is the same rule stated for one consumer.","override":"If a line states the Soul carries no IdentityBindingCredential until a new one is issued after finalization."}
{"gap":"E-joint-6150","affects":"the two quarantine vocabularies DeviceIncarnation.status and TemporalHealthState","rule":"The two vocabularies are different domains and compose by conjunction, not by mapping. DeviceIncarnation.status is the incarnation's identity and authority state; TemporalHealthState is the body's time-health state. quarantined or excluded on the incarnation withdraws authority outright, and the section 14.8 health gate applies to a body that is still an active incarnation.","grounds":[6150,6018],"why":"6150 shows quarantined and excluded as members of the incarnation status enum, which is the authority domain; the health gate at 6018 admits a fixed allowlist for a body that is not healthy, so it presumes an incarnation that is still recognized. An incarnation already quarantined is refused before the health gate is reached.","override":"If a line equates the two vocabularies or defines a mapping between their values."}
{"gap":"E-joint-3575","affects":"the PurchaseCandidates set of section 7.20.1 against the canonical Candidates derivation of section 13.10","rule":"PurchaseCandidates EXTENDS the canonical Candidates and therefore inherits every canonical term, including S != publisher(a) and the ActiveMembers membership term. The conditions listed at 7.20.1 are additions (a purchaser must have a payment path, must not already hold the asset), not replacements. Where the displayed formula omits an inherited term, the canonical term still applies.","grounds":[3575,5544],"why":"3575 introduces the set as 既存 Candidates を拡張して次で定義する — extension, not redefinition. A set defined by extending another cannot silently drop the extended set's terms; if the intent were replacement the word would be 再定義.","override":"If a line states PurchaseCandidates replaces the canonical derivation term by term."}
{"gap":"E-joint-9755","affects":"the section 20.7.7 storage GC gate and the section 19.2 PaymentState facts","rule":"Payment reversal does NOT enter the storage GC gate. The four GC conditions govern durable data deletion — valid replacement receipt, retention grace, tombstone authority, active references non-existence. A payment reversal is a commerce-state fact whose remedy is PayoutRecoveryAction and entitlement resettlement, not content deletion. If a reversal leaves content unreferenced, the existing reference test already covers it.","grounds":[9754,9755],"why":"The four conditions are all statements about replicas, references and tombstones, none about payment state. Section 19.2 keeps PaymentState independent of FulfillmentState, so a payment fact cannot be a precondition for a storage fact without breaking that independence.","override":"If a line adds payment reversal to the GC gate conditions."}
{"gap":"E-joint-9650","affects":"the retrievability instrument of section 20.5 and the audit instrument of section 20.7.6","rule":"The two schemes are different instruments, not duplicates. StorageChallenge/StorageAvailabilityProof is the RETRIEVABILITY check: a provider proves it can return a specified chunk at the challenge instant, per challenge and ephemeral. StorageAuditChallenge/StorageAuditResult is the AUDIT instrument: a durable, scored fact. Contribution is credited from the audit result; the retrievability proof is not itself a scoring input.","grounds":[9647,9650,13668],"why":"Section 20.5 opens by stating its own purpose — 保存の確認は、providerが挑戦時点に指定chunkを返せることを確かめる — which is the retrievability question, not the audit question. The storage contribution is computed from receipts, audits, references, failure domains and policy, so the audit object is the scoring channel.","override":"If a line states one instrument supersedes the other."}
{"gap":"E-joint-9467","affects":"the provider operator credential required at fulfillment time by section 19.4","rule":"The provider-credential requirement is evaluated AT FULFILLMENT TIME, not continuously from order time. If the credential has lapsed when fulfillment is attempted, the fulfillment is refused and the order moves to its failure and refund path under 18.13. The order is not voided retroactively, because the buyer-side gates at order time and the provider-side gates at fulfillment time are separate predicates.","grounds":[9466,8480],"why":"9466 places the credential requirement on the ServiceFulfillment provider for the promised service_kind, which is a predicate on the fulfillment act. The specification does not state a continuous-validity requirement across the order-to-fulfillment interval.","override":"If a line requires the credential to be held continuously from order to fulfillment."}
{"gap":"E-joint-9685","affects":"the bank provider registry update after a forum root succession","rule":"Root succession does not rewrite the registry object. The update is a NEW registry object whose authority chain terminates at the new root, appended like any other content-addressed object.
```
