The prior object remains valid for the period it governed, and readers select the newest object whose authority chain is finalized.","grounds":[9685,9683],"why":"9685 requires the post-succession registry update to carry ForumRootSuccession's finalized root authority chain, which is exactly what makes it a succession-authorized addition rather than an edit. Section 20.2 forbids rewriting or re-signing existing content-addressed objects in place, and an append with a new authority chain satisfies both.","override":"If a line states the bank provider registry is mutable in place."}
```jsonl
{"gap":"E-joint-2291","affects":"the genesis freeze of section 7.14 over the publication coefficient set","rule":"The genesis freeze covers the publication coefficient set as well as the cross-forum ratio. publication_base_bps and the six *_weight_bps are part of the declared profile, so an AssetPublicationIncentivePolicy update cannot retroactively invalidate an already-declared incentive_compatible_profile.","grounds":[2291,2296,2298],"why":"2296-2298 state the rule in general form: the policy version chain must not change or reinterpret declared_cross_forum_discount_ratio, an Owner threshold update applies only to future AssetAccess_direct evaluations, and an already-declared profile's conformance must not change while the forum does nothing. The publication coefficients enter the same declared profile and the same score_headroom, so the same constitutional ground at 2.4 applies.","override":"If a line states the publication coefficients are mutable after declaration."}
```

### 4.2 Rules this design supplies for operations the specification names and never defines

The rows in section 3 are grounded, one by one, on the line that establishes them. Eight are not.
Each of the eight names an act the specification uses — in an allowlist, in a denylist, in a
prohibition, or in a sentence that sends the reader to a procedure it never states — and no line
anywhere states it. Every one was found by searching for the ACT and not for the name, because
this registry invents most of its names and a search for a name proves nothing.

Two of the eight are one act under two names, and the second is recorded here as an alias rather
than counted as an operation. The other six divide into three shapes, and the shape decides what
has to be written down:

| shape | the rows | what the specification gives | what this design supplies |
|---|---|---|---|
| a procedure it sends the reader to and never states | RequestRevalidation, ReceiveReinstatementProof, CLOCK_RECOVERY | parameters and states, and the assertion that the procedure exists | the acts, in order, and the gate each runs under |
| a prohibition that presupposes an act | CreateAuthorityDelegation, ExecuteStorageGarbageCollection | what the act may not do | the act, and what the prohibitions fix about its scope |
| a mode of a procedure whose objects are stated | FetchRecoveryMaterial, SubmitTransferResolutionEvidence | the objects, and the exception that admits them | the act that produces them, and its authorising path |

Each row below is one decision. `act` is what is performed and by whom. `rule` is the procedure
or the bound this design adopts. `grounds` are the lines the rule rests on. `spec_name` is the
string the specification actually uses for it, which is not always the kind, and `presupposition`
is the line carrying that string — the checker requires the string to occur on that line, so a
rule cannot rest on lines that never mention the thing it governs. `why` is why those lines
support it, and `override` is the fact that would overturn it. `disposition` is `stated_here`
for a rule this design states, or `alias` for a name that is another row's act, in which case
`covers` names the rule that governs it.

The grounds are line numbers and are checked on every run: each must be a line of the
specification that carries text, and every rule must be named by a row whose evidence cites one
of its grounds, so the rule and the row cannot drift apart. A row governed by a rule here carries
no `defining_section`: a row whose section does not define it may not claim one.

```jsonl
{"kind":"RequestRevalidation","disposition":"stated_here","spec_name":"RequestRevalidation","presupposition":6656,"act":"A body whose health state is not healthy asks a fresh set of time witnesses to re-establish its time evidence.","rule":"Revalidation is the health-restoring path of sections 14.5 and 14.6, and it runs in the same shape as the original health observation: revalidation_observer_count observations from distinct required_observer_domains, gathered inside revalidation_success_window, after which the body state is revalidated and a new TemporalHealthLease may be issued. Requesting it restores no authority by itself: while the state is not healthy the body accrues no time and issues no ordinary object (5988), and the request is one of the operations section 14.4 requires to stay permitted while a body is unhealthy.","grounds":[5904,5926,5927,5985,5986,6656],"why":"5904 requires the operations needed for time-witness requests, time synchronisation, revalidation and recovery to stay permitted while a body is unhealthy, which presupposes a revalidation request; 5926 and 5927 fix its two parameters, an observer count and a success window, which mean nothing unless the body asks for observations; 5985 names the state the request puts the body into and 5986 the state it leaves by; 6656 is the freeze allowlist, which keeps the operation permitted while every other operation is refused.","override":"the specification states a revalidation procedure, or gaia-core and gaia-time fix a different observer set or success window."}
{"kind":"ReceiveReinstatementProof","disposition":"stated_here","spec_name":"ReceiveReinstatementProof","presupposition":6660,"act":"The party that governs a body health accepts the recovery evidence and records the body as revalidated.","rule":"Receiving the reinstatement proof is the acceptance half of the recovery hand-off whose submission half is SubmitRecoveryProof, and it is not a separate protocol object. The proof is the evidence the recovering side presents; accepting it is what satisfies the condition at 6523 that the new body meets health revalidation, and what moves the state to revalidated (5986), which authorises a new TemporalHealthLease. The receiver is the side that fixes and evaluates the forum temporal_health_policy, because the health state is per-forum (section 14.5); the succession switch itself is the Soul-Bank act the row PerformLostBodySuccession records.","grounds":[5986,6033,6520,6523,6660],"why":"6033 and 6660 keep the operation permitted while a body is unhealthy and during a transfer freeze, which places it on the receiving side of a recovery rather than on the acting side; 6523 makes the new body meeting health revalidation one of the conditions the succession switch requires, so something accepts that evidence and records the state; 5986 names the state so recorded; 6520 establishes that the switch is a separate act with its own conditions.","override":"the specification names a distinct reinstatement-proof object or a distinct receiver, or section 16.5 is revised so the succession switch evaluates the evidence without recording a state."}
{"kind":"CreateAuthorityDelegation","disposition":"stated_here","spec_name":"CreateAuthorityDelegation","presupposition":6677,"act":"A Soul current controller signs a bounded grant that lets a named authority key act in its place, within a stated scope and until a stated expiry, without moving the Soul controller or its identity key.","rule":"Authority delegation is the bounded-scope grant of the current controller authority to another authority key. Its scope is fixed by what the specification refuses: it is a normal-authority operation, so it is refused wherever normal authority is refused — after a Soul Transfer when signed with the identity key alone (6122) and during a forum root transfer freeze (6677) — and it is never a route to moving a Soul, because 6118 forbids one key serving as both identity and authority and 6116 binds every authority key to a Soul through a DeviceIncarnation. Delegation therefore changes which key may sign for a Soul within the granted scope; it changes neither the controller, nor the identity key, nor the Soul id.","grounds":[5044,6049,6116,6118,6122,6677],"why":"6677 forbids the operation during a forum root transfer freeze and 6049 during unhealthiness, and 6122 refuses it after a Soul Transfer when signed with the identity key alone — three independent prohibitions, each of which presupposes the act exists; 6116 and 6118 fix what the act may not do to the Soul identity, which is the boundary the grant must respect; 5044 shows the specification treating delegation as a distinct source of authority that a recommendation does not supply.","override":"the specification states a delegation procedure, or section 15.1 is revised to fix its scope and expiry."}
{"kind":"SubmitTransferResolutionEvidence","disposition":"stated_here","spec_name":"SubmitTransferResolutionEvidence","presupposition":6658,"act":"A party to a disputed Soul Transfer submits to the dispute resolver the evidence on which the resolution is decided.","rule":"Section 16.7.6 fixes two cancellation modes before finalization — mutual signature, or authorised dispute resolution (6791) — and states the objects the second mode appends, SoulTransferDispute and SoulTransferResolution (6789). Submitting the evidence is the first act of that second mode and is distinct from the resolution itself: the submission carries the material, and the resolver decision is the act that appends SoulTransferResolution. After finalization the same section forbids a rollback driven by a chargeback alone (6793) and requires an explicit resolution, eKYC and a new authority switch for any authority rollback (6794), which bounds what the evidence can do: it can support a resolution, it cannot by itself reverse a finalized controller.","grounds":[6658,6789,6791,6792,6793,6794],"why":"6791 states the two cancellation modes and so presupposes a submission of material for the second; 6789 names the two objects the resolution appends, separating the evidence from the resolution; 6792, 6793 and 6794 fix what a resolution may and may not reverse, which is the contract the submitted evidence operates under; 6658 is the freeze allowlist that keeps the operation permitted while a transfer is frozen, which is exactly when a dispute is live.","override":"the specification states an evidence-submission procedure, or names a resolver other than the dispute resolver 6791 refers to."}
{"kind":"CLOCK_RECOVERY","disposition":"stated_here","spec_name":"clock recovery","presupposition":14128,"act":"A body whose clock evidence is missing or expired re-establishes a verified time interval outside an established application session.","rule":"Clock recovery is the one pre-session exception the specification names four times and never defines: 6020 requires the control communication that makes it possible to be preserved as an allowlisted exception; 14039 and 14132 allow an existing clock recovery message before a SessionBinding is established; 14128 sends a handshake whose proof bundle is insufficient to the existing clock recovery procedure; and 15860 resolves an unknown or expired proof outside the handshake bundle by an explicit clock recovery or an existing trust anchor. CLOCK_RECOVERY is therefore the pre-session procedure that re-establishes a VerifiedTimeInterval for a body that cannot present one, using the time-witness path of chapter 14 under the forum TimeWitnessPolicy. It is bounded by the same prohibitions as the handshake: 14128 forbids treating it as a general private fetch, and 15860 forbids adding a general private object fetch to the pre-handshake exception.","grounds":[6020,14039,14128,14132,15860,15862],"why":"14128 calls it an existing procedure, which is a claim that it exists while no line states it; 15860 sends the reader to it again to resolve a proof the handshake bundle cannot carry; 14039, 14132 and 15862 keep an allowed clock recovery message inside the minimal pre-session exception, so the procedure is performed before any session exists; 6020 preserves the control communication it needs as an allowlisted exception to the unhealthiness prohibition.","override":"the specification defines the clock recovery procedure, or section 29.3 is revised to fix it inside the handshake rather than as a separate pre-session path."}
{"kind":"ExecuteStorageGarbageCollection","disposition":"stated_here","spec_name":"GC","presupposition":9755,"act":"A storage provider deletes durable data that has been superseded, once every guard the specification places on that deletion holds.","rule":"The specification states the garbage-collection gate and not the collection: 9755 forbids deleting durable data until a valid replacement receipt, the retention grace, tombstone authority and the non-existence of active references all hold, and 9754 forbids beginning with a deletion at all, requiring repair to start additively and refusing to delete a replica for suspicion, partition or temporary failure alone. The act is therefore the provider reclamation of superseded durable data, and the four conditions are its contract rather than a step of it. It is performed by the storage provider under storage_provider_authorization and produces the StorageReceipt the row already names. The rule recorded at 4.1 as E-joint-9755 fixes the other half of the same line: a payment reversal does not enter this gate.","grounds":[9750,9751,9753,9754,9755],"why":"9755 forbids a deletion, which presupposes a deleting act; 9754 forbids a deletion order and demands that repair start additively, which fixes the relation between reclamation and repair; 9750, 9751 and 9753 fix the durability classes the reclaimed data belongs to, which is the material the gate governs.","override":"the specification states a garbage-collection procedure, or section 20.7 is revised to make reclamation part of TriggerDurableRepair rather than an operation of its own."}
{"kind":"RunStorageGarbageCollection","disposition":"alias","covers":"ExecuteStorageGarbageCollection","spec_name":"GC","presupposition":9755,"act":"The same act ExecuteStorageGarbageCollection records; this name is an alias for it.","rule":"This name and ExecuteStorageGarbageCollection are one act. The row is kept as an excluded row rather than deleted, so that a reader who searches for either name finds the other, and the rule stated for ExecuteStorageGarbageCollection governs both. Its disposition is excluded because it is not a second Core Operation — the registry keeps the name, not a second operation.","grounds":[9754,9755],"why":"Both names are grounded on the one line that governs storage reclamation, 9755, and the specification gives neither name: it writes GC. Two registry rows therefore name one act, and the second is recorded as an alias rather than counted as an operation.","override":"the specification distinguishes the two names, or the registry is revised to drop the alias."}
{"kind":"AcceptRecoveryEligibilityCredential","disposition":"stated_here","spec_name":"RecoveryEligibilityCredential","presupposition":6522,"act":"The eKYC service produces a Soul's recovery-eligibility credential — an Owner-authorized eKYC issuer issues a RecoveryEligibilityCredential binding the subject Soul and its identity-pubkey commitment — and the recovery side admits that credential as the proof on which a lost-body succession may proceed.","rule":"The design adopts the credential as a section 17.5 artifact: it is issued by an eKYC service holding a valid Owner-authorized EkycServiceAuthorization, carries subject_soul_id, subject_identity_pubkey_commitment, recovery_scope, verified_at, issued_at, expire and a revocation_status_ref, and must be unexpired and non-revoked at the evaluation time. Admission is allowed only as an input to the lost-body succession switch of section 16.5, and the credential creates no Q, depth, early-access slot or seed. No procedure for the credential's production or admission is stated anywhere, so the design states one here.","grounds":[6522,7007,7010,7025],"why":"6522 makes a valid Owner-authorized eKYC-issued RecoveryEligibilityCredential an admission condition of the lost-body switch, so the specification uses the act only as a condition; 7007 states the credential is issued by an Owner-authorized eKYC issuer and is required to apply for lost-body succession; 7010 fixes the credential's canonical fields, so its production is a real object creation rather than a bare mention; 7025 bounds what the credential may not create, which presupposes that it is produced and admitted.","override":"A line stating who accepts the credential and the acceptance procedure, or a section defining a distinct recovery-eligibility-credential admission object."}
{"kind":"SubmitNeedMergeAttestation","disposition":"stated_here","spec_name":"NeedMergeAttestation","presupposition":11480,"act":"Each validator of the epoch's distributed Civic validator committee evaluates a NeedMergeProposal with its own local means and issues a signed NeedMergeAttestation recording its decision to accept or reject the merge.","rule":"The design adopts the attestation as the validator's own local judgement of a NeedMergeProposal, issued as a NeedMergeAttestation whose decision is accept or reject. An attestation is counted only when its validator is in CivicValidatorSet(F,e) and is not excluded for conflict of interest, at most one per validator; the merge reaches finality only when the counted accept attestations reach 2f+1 and a valid CivicFinalityCertificate exists. The evaluator's LLM, embedding, model identifier, API provider and prompt may not enter the consensus validity predicate.","grounds":[11480,12089,12100,12106],"why":"12089 states that each validator automatically evaluates the merge proposal with local means but never states that it issues its attestation; 11480 defines NeedMergeAttestation only as the record of each validator's accept or reject, which presupposes the act; 12106 and 12100 use the accept attestations as counted inputs to the 2f+1 quorum, so the act must exist and be bounded but is nowhere stated.","override":"A line stating a validator submitting or issuing its NeedMergeAttestation, or a section defining that submission."}
{"kind":"SubmitTransferDispute","disposition":"stated_here","spec_name":"SubmitTransferDispute","presupposition":6657,"act":"A party to a Soul Transfer, or an authorized dispute resolver acting for it, submits a SoulTransferDispute recording the objective grounds on which the transfer is contested, the submitter, the target agreement, the time and whether the freeze continues.","rule":"The design adopts the submission as an append-only dispute object, admitted while a transfer freeze is in force and included in the transfer-safe operation set of section 16.7.2. It may be submitted before finalization or within the stated dispute window and never deletes history; the transfer cannot finalize while an unresolved dispute exists, and the dispute state is committed in transfer_dispute_registry_root. The submission is transfer-gated rather than authority-gated, since the freeze gate permits it while it withholds every normal-authority operation.","grounds":[6657,6789,6753,784],"why":"6657 lists SubmitTransferDispute in the transfer-freeze allowlist, so the specification uses the act while stating only that it is permitted; 6789 states that a Soul Transfer cancellation is not a history deletion and appends the dispute object, presupposing the submission; 6753 makes NoUnresolvedTransferDispute a conjunct of CanFinalizeSoulTransfer and 784 fixes a transfer_dispute_registry_root, so the act must produce an addressable dispute state.","override":"A line stating a party submitting a SoulTransferDispute, or a section defining the submission procedure."}
{"kind":"SubmitLostBodySuccessionRequest","disposition":"stated_here","spec_name":"LostBodySuccessionRequest","presupposition":6505,"act":"A recovering party submits a signed LostBodySuccessionRequest naming the lost incarnation, the successor incarnation and authority key, the successor health proof, the recovery-eligibility credential, the recovery epoch and requester time window, a recovery commitment and the requester signature.","rule":"The design adopts the submission as the request object of section 16.5, handled only as an exception to the normal authority gate. A request may be admitted only when all six conditions of section 16.5 hold — a valid Owner-authorized eKYC RecoveryEligibilityCredential, successor health revalidation, a Soul not in succession_contested, an elapsed cooldown and dispute window, the recovery-policy threshold, and no competing successor for the same lost incarnation. If two requests reference the same lost incarnation the Soul moves to succession_contested and normal authority is zero until the contest is resolved.","grounds":[6502,6505,6522,6529,6533],"why":"6502 states only that Soul-Bank may perform a lost-body succession, and the request object of 6505 is declared as a type with no line stating its submission; 6522 and the remaining conditions make the request an admission condition of the switch; 6529 presupposes competing requests; 6533 adds the recovery-policy path, so the act exists and is bounded but is never stated.","override":"A line stating who submits the LostBodySuccessionRequest and the submission procedure."}
{"kind":"RefundMaturityBond","disposition":"stated_here","spec_name":"MaturityBond","presupposition":1700,"act":"The Payment-Service refunds the reserved amount of a MaturityBond when the recipient's independent maturity is not established before the bond's expire, the alternative being that the bond lapses with no return.","rule":"The design adopts the bond's failure branch as a bounded refund: a MaturityBond binds issuer, recipient, forum, the community-certificate or application session, amount, currency, expire and the on-failure refund-or-lapse rule. On a confirmed failure the reserved amount is returned to the payer and the bond is extinguished, or it expires with no return; the bond may never be an input to Q, depth, CanIssue, CanIssueTo, A_seed, AssetScore, any bonus, Candidates or Reach. The refund is a payment-layer act and changes no maturity value.","grounds":[1700,1702,4061,4062],"why":"1700 states that the bond binds an on-failure refund-or-lapse rule and pays the issuer only when the recipient's independent maturity is shown, so the specification uses the refund act while never stating it; 1702 and 4061-4062 fix what the bond is bound to and forbid it from feeding any maturity value, which bounds the otherwise-unstated act.","override":"A line stating the refund procedure for a MaturityBond, or a statement that the bond has no refund branch."}
{"kind":"SubmitRecoveryProof","disposition":"stated_here","spec_name":"SubmitRecoveryProof","presupposition":6031,"act":"A body whose authority is lost or unhealthy, or the recovering party on its behalf, submits the recovery proof that its recovery procedure produced, so that the recovery can be evaluated and a new authority body activated.","rule":"The design treats the submission as an operation permitted while a body is not healthy, drawn from the allowed set of section 14.8. The proof is the evidence the recovery path presents — the recovery-eligibility credential, the successor health-revalidation proof and the recovery commitment of section 16.5 — and submitting it changes no protocol state by itself: it is evaluated by the lost-body succession switch, which is a separate operation. A body that is not healthy may submit it, and it may not bypass the switch.","grounds":[6031,6520,6523,6533],"why":"6031 lists SubmitRecoveryProof in the AllowedWhenNotHealthy allowlist, so the specification uses the act while stating only that it is permitted; 6520 and 6523 make the lost-body switch conditional on the successor's health revalidation, which the proof evidences; 6533 shows the recovery-policy path the proof serves. No line states the submission procedure or its object.","override":"A line stating the SubmitRecoveryProof procedure or its object, or a section defining it."}
{"kind":"ConfigureHeatStabilizer","disposition":"stated_here","spec_name":"HeatStabilizerConfiguration","presupposition":11105,"act":"The Heat Stabilizer policy authority issues a signed HeatStabilizerConfiguration fixing the mode, the enabled flag, the global and forum kill switches, the rollout cohort, the observation windows, the thresholds, the caps, the weights and the validity interval, and that configuration is the effective one for every forum until a later configuration or a signed rollback replaces it.","rule":"The design adopts the configuration as the object of section 22.7 and admits it only when it is canonical, signed by the configured Heat Stabilizer policy authority, inside its validity interval, internally bounded and linked to a valid HeatStabilizerPolicy; an invalid configuration fails closed to observe_only. A signed HeatStabilizerRollback may restore a prior configuration or force observe_only, and a valid rollback or kill switch takes priority over an ordinary soft-intervention configuration. The first production configuration is mode observe_only, enabled false, global_kill_switch true and zero caps, and soft_intervention requires a calibration release record, an explicit finite rollout cohort and a tested rollback path.","grounds":[5213,5245,5247,11105,13999],"why":"5247 states only a validity boundary and a fail-closed requirement, qualifying the configure operation and naming no act; 5245 presupposes that configurations are made and replaced when it says a signed rollback may restore a prior configuration; 5213 presupposes an active signed configuration when it bars soft_intervention without one; 13999 presupposes later configurations when it says a later configuration may enable observe_only and that soft_intervention may be enabled only after a documented calibration release decision. The specification uses the act throughout and states neither who configures nor the procedure.","override":"A line stating that the policy authority signs and publishes a configuration, or a section defining the configuration act and its order of events."}
{"kind":"CreateAssetRecord","disposition":"stated_here","spec_name":"AssetRecord","presupposition":1792,"act":"The publisher registers the first version of an AssetRecord for a ProtectedContent, fixing value, protected_content_ref, publisher_pubkey, required_access_threshold, price, currency, active and the asset_lineage_id, which is derived from the publisher key, the first protected_content_ref, created_at and the asset_creation_nonce that gaia-core generates.","rule":"The design adopts the record as the section 10.6 object: the first version's asset_lineage_id is the hash of the publisher public key, the first protected_content_ref, created_at and gaia-core's asset_creation_nonce, and the publisher must not choose the nonce. Consensus-critical fields are never overwritten; an update creates a new record hash, deactivates the old version and activates the new one, and a version chain with a branch, a gap, a time reversal or a same-tick reference is rejected as TemporalCycle or EquivocatedAssetVersion. The record's value enters A_real only after the section 13.9 review by two distinct qualified reviewers other than the publisher.","grounds":[1792,1794,5525,5534,14181],"why":"1792 states the registration and versioning rule and the prohibition on overwriting, but its subject is the asset rather than an actor and it gives no order of events; 1794 presupposes the creation when it fixes how the first version's asset_lineage_id is derived and forbids the publisher to specify gaia-core's asset_creation_nonce; 5525 and 5534 govern the value review that makes the record active without narrating the creation; 14181 carries CreateAssetRecord only as a Conformance Matrix example fixing its mutation, TimeHandshake, interface and idempotency contract. No line narrates a publisher creating the record.","override":"A line stating the publisher submitting an AssetRecord and the creation procedure, or a section defining the creation act."}
{"kind":"CreateMaturityBond","disposition":"stated_here","spec_name":"MaturityBond","presupposition":1700,"act":"A forum's payment layer creates a MaturityBond bound to a community-issuance session or to a community certificate, naming the issuer, the recipient, the forum, the amount, the currency, the expiry and the on-failure refund-or-lapse rule, and the bond pays the issuer only when a finalized checkpoint shows the recipient independently matured on grounds other than the issuer.","rule":"The design adopts the bond as the payment-layer surety of section 7.6.1: a forum may place a MaturityBondPolicy in the payment layer, and the bond binds issuer, recipient, forum, the community certificate or application session, amount, currency, expire and the on-failure refund-or-lapse rule. It pays the issuer only when a finalized checkpoint shows IndependentEligible(S,F,d_cult;I) and Q_{-I} >= 1. It is never an input to Q, depth, CanIssue, CanIssueTo, A_seed, AssetScore, CultivationBonus, CitationBonus, Candidates or Reach, and a forum that uses no bond declares MaturityBond = 0.","grounds":[1700,1702,4061,4062],"why":"1700 states what the bond binds and when it pays, and permits a forum to place the policy, but never states who creates the bond or in what order; 1702 forbids the bond from feeding any maturity value, which bounds the otherwise unstated act; 4061 and 4062 restate the binding fields and that prohibition in the implementation-invariant list, so the specification uses the creation act as a constraint while never stating it.","override":"A line stating who creates a MaturityBond and the creation procedure, or a section defining the creation act."}
{"kind":"ExpireGaiaServiceCredit","disposition":"stated_here","spec_name":"GaiaServiceCreditExpiration","presupposition":8456,"act":"The Payment-Service extinguishes the unused units of a GaiaServiceCreditGrant once its expires_at has passed, and records the event as a signed GaiaServiceCreditExpiration binding the grant, the expired time, the unused units, the disposition extinguished and the payment-service authorization.","rule":"The design adopts the expiration as the section 18.12.5 object: on reaching expires_at the unused units are extinguished, the conservation granted_units = consumed_units + expired_units + remaining_units continues to hold, and the event is the signed GaiaServiceCreditExpiration carrying grant_ref, expired_at, unused_units, disposition extinguished and the payment-service authorization. Expired credit is never restored or converted into a fiat payout right, a PayoutEntitlement, another Credit, AssetScore or another Soul's rights. Extinguishing the credit does not by itself reduce the source PayoutEntitlement's principal or claimable amount.","grounds":[8379,8418,8453,8456,8467],"why":"8453 states the consequence, that unused units are extinguished past expires_at, and the schema that follows, while 8379 states only that the Payment-Service may grant the credit under an Owner-authorized GaiaServiceCreditPolicy; no line narrates the Payment-Service emitting the expiration object. 8418 makes credit redemption not reduce the source entitlement and 8467 forbids restoring or converting expired credit, both presupposing that the expiration happens while stating no procedure.","override":"A line stating who expires the credit and the expiration procedure, or a section defining the expiry act."}
{"kind":"RegisterAssetLineageNode","disposition":"stated_here","spec_name":"AssetLineageNode","presupposition":3248,"act":"A publisher registers an asset that has a parent as an AssetLineageNode — a node in a single-parent directed chain to the origin asset, carrying a unique parent reference reachable to the origin, the inherited origin policy hash, a generation_from_origin and a lineage depth.","rule":"The design adopts the eight conditions of section 7.18.2 as the registration contract: the parent resolves canonically and is active, the parent's commercial-rights policy allows the child relationship kind, an AssetRightsGrant from parent to child is valid or the policy is explicitly self-executing, the origin policy hash is inherited, lineage_depth = parent.lineage_depth + 1 and <= max_lineage_depth, the parent chain contains no cycle, and every ancestor hash commits in canonical order. A failing condition is rejected as InvalidAssetLineage, AssetLineageCycle, MissingParentAsset, UnauthorizedCommercialDerivative or AssetLineageDepthExceeded.","grounds":[3248,3252,3254,3263],"why":"3248 requires an ALR-subject asset to form an AssetLineageNode carrying a unique parent reference reachable to the origin asset, so the specification uses the node as a requirement on the asset; 3252-3263 fix the eight validity conditions and the reject codes that govern it; no line names who registers the node or in what order, so the registration act itself is never stated.","override":"A line stating who registers an AssetLineageNode, or a section defining the registration procedure."}
{"kind":"RevokePaymentServiceAuthorization","disposition":"stated_here","spec_name":"PaymentServiceAuthorization","presupposition":8682,"act":"Owner threshold revokes a Payment-Service authorization — the Owner-threshold-signed PaymentServiceAuthorization that authorized the Payment-Service operating and signing keys and fixed the Stripe rail.","rule":"The design adopts revocation as an Owner-threshold act that ends the authorization for future use: the Owner threshold issues a revocation referencing the authorization, after which it may no longer sign new PaymentReceipts or authorize new operations. The effect is bounded — a central service's revocation takes effect only for new issuance and new authority and never retrospectively invalidates state already verified.","grounds":[7320,8682,8712,7409],"why":"7320 lists 'Payment-Service運営鍵の認可、失効、緊急停止' among the Owner-key roles and 8682 lists Payment-Service revocation among the acts Owner threshold is permitted to perform, so the specification uses the act in an allowlist; 7409 states only the counterpart authorization by which the operating and signing keys receive Owner threshold authorization, and no line states the revocation act or its object; 8712 bounds the effect of a central service's revocation, presupposing that it happens.","override":"A line stating who revokes a PaymentServiceAuthorization, the revocation object, or the revocation procedure."}
{"kind":"RevokeRootEkycProvider","disposition":"stated_here","spec_name":"root eKYC provider","presupposition":7324,"act":"Owner threshold revokes a root eKYC provider's authorization — the RootEkycProviderAuthorization by which the provider was first certified.","rule":"The design adopts the revocation as an Owner-threshold act that withdraws the provider's authority for future credential issuance, mirroring the initial certification, taking effect only for new issuance and new authority. It must not retrospectively invalidate credentials, certificates or forum state already verified, and the provider's self-eKYC prohibition and scope rules continue to bound what it may sign until the revocation takes effect.","grounds":[7324,8682,9769,8712],"why":"7324 lists 'root eKYC providerおよびeKYC serviceの認可、失効' among the Owner-key roles and 8682 repeats it, so the specification uses the revocation in an allowlist; 9769 states only the initial certification ('初回認定される'), the counterpart act, and no line states the revocation act or its object; 8712 bounds the effect of a revocation.","override":"A line stating who revokes a root eKYC provider, the revocation object, or the revocation procedure."}
{"kind":"SetAssetPublicationIncentivePolicy","disposition":"stated_here","spec_name":"AssetPublicationIncentivePolicy","presupposition":3837,"act":"A forum's founder sets an AssetPublicationIncentivePolicy — on the forum genesis or on a backward-compatible policy object — fixing publication_base_bps, the six *_weight_bps applied to value(a), the age/freshness/quorum/failure-domain parameters, and the citation and mediated-cultivation extension caps.","rule":"The design adopts the policy as set at forum genesis or on a later backward-compatible policy object, with genesis/policy validation enforcing that asset_citation_extension_cap_bps <= 120 and asset_mediated_cultivation_extension_cap_bps <= 100; a violating object is rejected as ResourceLimitExceeded. No A_max-proportional per-asset cap and no per-count cap may be placed on the publication path.","grounds":[3837,3827,3857,3816],"why":"3837 permits setting the policy on a forum genesis or a backward-compatible policy object and enumerates its mandatory fields, but names no actor and states no order; 3827 and 3857 fix the protocol hard caps, the basis-point sum condition and the rejection, bounding the policy whose setting the design must supply; 3816 opens the parameters section that carries those caps.","override":"A line naming who sets the AssetPublicationIncentivePolicy and the setting procedure."}
{"kind":"StorageAuditResponse","disposition":"stated_here","spec_name":"StorageAuditResult","presupposition":9722,"act":"A durable storage provider answers a StorageAuditChallenge by returning the requested chunk or range, and the answer is committed and signed so that a StorageAuditResult records the response (response_commitment, verified_bytes, provider_soul_id, provider_signature) together with a verifier's result.","rule":"The design adopts the response as the provider's answer to a challenge issued against a StorageReceipt: the requested chunk or range is returned, bound to the challenge nonce and receipt, and a provider-signed StorageAuditResult commits the response, which is independently verified against the object's ciphertext hash and manifest. Audit success counts only after that match; failure starts at-risk/repair and decays the contribution.","grounds":[9722,9647,9744,9671],"why":"9722 lists a within-deadline StorageAuditResult as one of the only valid evidences of capacity, so the specification uses the response as evidence; 9647 states only what the retrieval check confirms (the provider returning the specified chunk) and 9671 defines StorageAvailabilityProof only as showing the chunk could be returned, but neither states who produces the StorageAuditResult or the response procedure; 9744 states the audit success/failure semantics, presupposing a response.","override":"A line stating the storage provider's response procedure or the production of a StorageAuditResult."}
{"kind":"EnableIndexDistribution","disposition":"stated_here","spec_name":"enableindexdistribution","presupposition":9516,"act":"An operator holding public-metadata update authority and passing the storage/index policy and the receipt/audit conditions publishes a signed public discovery index manifest, making that index available to discovery clients; by the same line, providing the index confers no individual right to send an advertisement.","rule":"The design adopts the act as the publication of a section 22.5 DiscoveryIndexManifest: the operator is a Soul under a valid authority, epoch lease and health lease, and the manifest namespaces public_discovery, commits forum_shard_commitment, checkpoint_set_root, the source asset and forum discovery root sets, the index schema and analyzer hashes, the segment object refs and the covered tick window, and is signed by signer_authority_pubkey. The manifest is committed under discovery_index_registry_root and carries the section 20.7.7 durability requirement (critical metadata including DiscoveryIndexManifest at strict R=3 in distinct durable failure domains) before it counts as available. Clients verify the manifest by signature, checkpoint and proof only, and an index carries no addressable or deliverable target set.","grounds":[9516,790,9750,15900],"why":"9516 is the only line that names the kind: it grants the executable class to `enableindexdistribution` on public-metadata update authority plus storage/index policy and receipt/audit conditions, and says an index confers no advertisement-sending right, so the specification treats the act as real while stating no procedure. 790 makes the index a checkpoint-committed object (discovery_index_registry_root holds DiscoveryIndexManifest and index segment references), 9750 imposes the R=3 durability requirement on it, and 15900 keeps it under Gaia's signature, checkpoint and proof regime rather than the network topic search.","override":"A line that names the publisher of the index manifest and fixes the moment of publication — for example an enumerated publish sequence for DiscoveryIndexManifest, or a sentence saying the index operator publishes the manifest after committing the segment objects — would make the act stated and the disposition recite."}
{"kind":"PurchasePromotion","disposition":"stated_here","spec_name":"purchasepromotion","presupposition":9511,"act":"A Soul holding payment authority that can create a PaymentIntent buys a paid boost for an existing AssetAnnouncement under a valid promotion policy, price, currency and payment rail; the Payment-Service settles the payment and the purchase passes through payment_pending, then paid, then an active PromotionGrant.","rule":"The design adopts the purchase as the creation of one PromotionGrant bound to a single AssetAnnouncement through a PaymentReceipt that fixes the target announcement, the publisher, the amount, the currency and the purchased display period, so a receipt bought for another announcement or another member cannot be diverted. The grant is valid only while ValidAnnouncement holds and promotion_started_at <= t < promotion_expires_at, and it carries sponsor_label_required=true. Settlement (Stripe cost, Gaia network fee, Forum Revenue Pool contribution and per-recipient allocation) belongs to PaymentSettlement, and the grant may never change AssetScore, Q, depth, CanIssue, CanIssueTo, A_seed, CultivationBonus, CitationBonus or Candidates.","grounds":[9511,4210,4237,4604],"why":"9511 names the kind and presupposes the act rather than stating it: it requires that the requester already hold payment authority and be able to create a PaymentIntent, then fixes only the resulting state order payment_pending to paid to PromotionGrant active, and warns that executable_now does not guarantee payment completion. 4210 and 4237 use the resulting object (a settled right against an existing AssetAnnouncement, with settlement performed by the Payment-Service), and 4604 says only that a paid boost likewise uses a PaymentReceipt fixing announcement, amount and period; no line states who initiates the purchase or what the purchase does step by step.","override":"An enumerated purchase sequence for a promotion, in the way section 10.10 enumerates the normal-asset and PressRoom-joining sequences, or a sentence naming the sponsor who creates the PaymentIntent and the order of the steps, would make the act stated and the disposition recite."}
{"kind":"RefreshDiscoveryMetadata","disposition":"stated_here","spec_name":"refreshdiscoverymetadata","presupposition":9516,"act":"The publisher of an asset, or the operator maintaining the checkpoint-committed public index, refreshes the asset's public discovery metadata by superseding its AssetDiscoveryRecord with a new signed record, or by putting a tombstone in the checkpoint when the asset has turned inactive, revoked or expired.","rule":"The design adopts the refresh as a new signed AssetDiscoveryRecord at a later discovery_metadata_version rather than an edit of the old record: it keeps asset_ref pointing at an active AssetRecord, forum_id at a forum whose active registry contains the asset, offer_refs at active ServiceOffers and visibility at public_discoverable, and it must expose an inclusion proof from the checkpoint's discovery registry root. When the asset turns inactive, revoked or expired, an updating record or a tombstone is committed into the checkpoint, and no title, summary, tag or hash may expose secrets, personal data, DEK material or plaintext hashes. The refresh changes no AssetScore and must never contradict the existing acceptance predicates of Payment-Service, ContentAccessGrant, PressRoom, membership or forum policy.","grounds":[9516,4663,10676],"why":"9516 grants the executable class to `refreshdiscoverymetadata` on public-metadata update authority plus storage/index policy and receipt/audit conditions and states no procedure, and it is the only line that names the kind. 4663 and 10676 state what an AssetDiscoveryRecord must satisfy and require an updating record or tombstone once the asset is inactive, revoked or expired — a rule on the record's content that presupposes the refresh without stating who performs it or in what order.","override":"A line naming the actor who refreshes the metadata and the order of the refresh — for example an enumerated update sequence, or a sentence that the publisher signs a new AssetDiscoveryRecord after each value or offer change — would make the act stated and the disposition recite."}
{"kind":"AcceptTransferDistinctnessCredential","disposition":"stated_here","spec_name":"TransferDistinctnessCredential","presupposition":6921,"act":"An eKYC provider that holds a transfer-scoped authorization issues a minimal-disclosure, signed TransferDistinctnessCredential for two Souls, and the transfer side admits that credential as the distinctness assertion on which DistinctLegalSubject and the transfer's acceptance depend.","rule":"The design adopts the credential as a section 17.2.1 artifact: an authorized eKYC service issues it with subject_a_soul_id, subject_b_soul_id, scope_commitment, the assertion distinct or prohibited_related_party or indeterminate, issued_at, expire, revocation_status_ref and the issuer authorization reference, and only assertion=distinct admits a transfer. It exists to keep plaintext personal data and beneficial-ownership data out of P2P objects, so the credential carries only a commitment and the assertion. A missing, expired, revoked or indeterminate credential is refused with the missing-eKYC and non-distinct rejection, and the credential can never substitute for the transfer's eKYC, freeze or payment checks.","grounds":[6921,6904,6939,4070],"why":"6921 states only that the eKYC provider can issue the credential and why, and no line states who requests it, when it is issued relative to the transfer, or how it is consumed. 6904 makes the seller and buyer distinct legal subjects, 6939 admits only the `distinct` assertion and refuses `indeterminate`, and 4070 refuses a Soul Transfer without a valid, unexpired transfer-purpose eKYC and DistinctLegalSubject — all of which use the credential as a condition and presuppose its acceptance without stating the act.","override":"A line stating the issuance or acceptance as a procedure — who asks the eKYC provider for the credential, when it is issued in the transfer sequence, and where in SoulTransferFinalization it is consumed — would make the act stated and the disposition recite."}
{"kind":"CreatePressRoom","disposition":"stated_here","spec_name":"PressRoom","presupposition":4440,"act":"A Soul creates a PressRoom — a logical distribution channel bound to one forum — by publishing a signed PressRoom object that fixes owner_pubkey, forum_id, name, description_ref, membership_mode, eligibility_rule, membership_price, currency, membership_duration, max_members, early_access_window and created_at.","rule":"The design adopts the creation as the publication of one signed section 10.7 PressRoom object under the creator's normal authority: the room is a logical distribution channel that takes the same ciphertext, ContentStore, key-envelope form and decryption path as ordinary assets, and it is committed under the forum checkpoint's pressroom_registry_root. A paid room (open_paid, invited_paid) requires the membership fee only after the non-monetary eligibility_rule, a free room (open_free, invited_free) must leave the fee fields unset, eligibility_rule is built only from the existing non-monetary conditions, and the room grants no key of its own — each detail is released through a ContentAccessGrant and a recipient-specific KeyEnvelope.","grounds":[4440,4476,4492,4594],"why":"4440 defines the PressRoom as a logical distribution channel and 4476 says what a room does (it grounds ContentAccessGrant issuance and gives no special key or content form), but no line states who creates one or in what order. 4492 introduces the PressRoomMembershipGrant and 4594 opens the joining sequence with an applicant sending a join request, both of which presuppose a room that already exists, and no enumerated creation sequence appears anywhere.","override":"A line stating that a Soul publishes a PressRoom object with its owner and channel fields, or an enumerated room-creation sequence comparable to the join sequence of section 10.10, would make the act stated and the disposition recite."}
{"kind":"IssueStorageChallenge","disposition":"stated_here","spec_name":"StorageChallenge","presupposition":9650,"act":"A challenger — the storage customer under a StorageContract, or an auditor acting for it — issues a signed StorageChallenge against that contract, fixing the object hash, a challenge nonce, the requested chunk index or range and an expiry, to test that the provider can still return the specified chunk.","rule":"The design adopts the challenge as the section 20.5 StorageChallenge issued under the challenger's own authority: it binds one contract_id, one object_hash and one nonce, carries chunk_index_or_range and expire, and is signed by the challenger, so a challenge that cannot be tied to a live contract or to an unexpired window is refused. Passing the challenge proves only that the chunk was returned at the challenge time and never future retention, so the challenge is complemented by the section 20.7.7 replication rules (strict R=3 in distinct durable failure domains, EC only under sufficient domain diversity). A challenge that cannot be verified against the contract's replica requirement is treated as an audit failure that starts at-risk and repair and decays the contribution.","grounds":[9647,9650,9671,13149,15894],"why":"9647 states only what the storage confirmation verifies and 9671 only what the answer shows; neither names the challenger, the moment of issue or an order. The objects appear otherwise only as a section 3.1 type, as section 20.5 and 22.5 schemas and as the InvalidStorageAuditChallenge reject code. 13149 derives the network-side valid contribution from StorageReceipt and StorageAudit alone, and 15894 denies that transport success is an audit success, so the challenge is presupposed by the accounting without being stated.","override":"A line naming the challenger and the moment of issue — for example that the customer issues a StorageChallenge before each proof_schedule slot, or an enumerated challenge-then-response sequence — would make the act stated and the disposition recite."}
{"kind":"RespondStorageChallenge","disposition":"stated_here","spec_name":"StorageAvailabilityProof","presupposition":9671,"act":"The provider named by the StorageContract answers an issued challenge by returning the specified chunk — or its commitment with a Merkle proof — signed as a StorageAvailabilityProof that binds the contract, the challenge hash, the object hash and the response time.","rule":"The design adopts the response as the section 20.5 StorageAvailabilityProof: the provider signs the answer, binds it to the exact challenge_hash and object_hash, and the answer counts only within the challenge's expire. The proof is deliberately limited — it shows the chunk was returned at the challenge time and nothing about future retention — so it is complemented by the R=3 replication, EC and repair rules and never serves as a durability claim. A missing, late, wrong-range or unbound answer is an audit failure: it starts at-risk and repair, decays the contribution and is refused by the InvalidStorageAuditResult and StorageAuditExpired reject codes.","grounds":[9671,9647,9744,13149],"why":"9671 states what the StorageAvailabilityProof shows and 9647 what the storage confirmation verifies, so the answer is characterised by its meaning; neither line names the responding provider as an actor performing a stated procedure, nor fixes an order of challenge then response. 9744 makes audit success valid only when checked against the object's ciphertext hash and manifest, and 13149 derives the network's view of storage contribution only from StorageReceipt and StorageAudit, so the response is used as evidence while never being stated as an act.","override":"A line stating the response as a procedure — that the provider must return the requested chunk with its Merkle proof within the challenge's expire, in an enumerated challenge-then-proof sequence — would make the act stated and the disposition recite."}
{"kind":"RecordAssetCitationContribution","disposition":"stated_here","spec_name":"AssetCitationContribution","presupposition":11302,"act":"A publisher-side authority records, as a signed AssetCitationContribution, that one published asset has been cited by or depends on another asset, so the credit can be evaluated into the cited asset's CitationBonus extension.","rule":"The design adopts the AssetCitationContribution object as the section 22.8 canonical schema fixes it: an authority-signed record keyed by cited_asset_ref, citing_asset_ref, cited and citing publisher Soul, forum and evidence checkpoint, whose citation_basis is asset_dependency | asset_lineage | citation_certificate | verified_reference | other and whose credit_state moves pending -> eligible -> credited | excluded | revoked under the cited asset's credited_epoch_id. It is recorded only against a finalized evaluation checkpoint and only for a citation that survives the section 23.24 independence predicate; the amount it supports is bounded by asset_citation_extension_cap_bps <= ASSET_CITATION_EXTENSION_CAP_BPS = 120 and applies to the cited asset's value(a), never to A_max.","grounds":[11302,3807,3879],"why":"11302 puts AssetCitationContribution among the publication-path objects evaluated against value(a) and forbids the A_max base, which presupposes a recorded contribution that is there to evaluate; 3807 states the asset-level citation extension and its 120 bps cap, so the act must produce an object that carries the extension; 3879 records that the implementation can create these publication objects, which presumes an act of creation. No line anywhere states who records the contribution or how.","override":"A line stating who records an AssetCitationContribution and the recording procedure, or a revision that makes the object a derived projection of evaluated evidence rather than a separately signed record."}
{"kind":"InitiateStorageRepair","disposition":"stated_here","spec_name":"repair","presupposition":9744,"act":"repair","rule":"The design adopts the repair as the storage provider's additive re-establishment of the replication the object's durability class requires. A provider holding a valid storage_provider_authorization and a durable participation mode — the only mode whose repair target is permitted (9705) — begins it on an at-risk trigger, which is an audit failure (9744) or an observed shortfall of the R=3 / EC requirement (9750-9753). The repair only ever ADDS a replica or a shard: it must not delete an existing replica on suspicion, partition or temporary failure alone (9754, 13639), and any later removal is governed by the GC gate of 9755 and nothing else. It runs against the object's ciphertext hash and manifest, not against a self-reported inventory (9744), and it emits the StorageReceipt and StorageAuditResult by which the trigger and the completion are recorded. It is performed after a SessionBinding exists: storage/audit/repair requests before the handshake are refused under PreHandshakeProtocolMessageForbidden (14132). Repair changes no maturity value; the contribution it restores is credited only from receipts and audits (13149).","grounds":[9744,9754,13156,13639,14132],"why":"9754 is a norm that a repair must start additively together with a prohibition that no replica be deleted for suspicion, partition or temporary failure — both presuppose the act and state only what it may not do; 9744 states the trigger and its effect on the contribution, where the subject is the event audit failure and not an actor; 13156 and 13639 use repair as one of the instruments availability rests on; 14132 uses it in a prohibition list; and 9701 permits a repair target only for durable nodes. No line states the actor, the object produced or an order, so the design states the procedure here.","override":"A line stating who initiates a storage repair, the object it produces and the order of its steps, or a section defining the repair act — in which case this rule is replaced by that section."}
{"kind":"SubmitSoulTransferDispute","disposition":"stated_here","spec_name":"SoulTransferDispute","presupposition":6789,"act":"SoulTransferDispute","rule":"The design adopts the submission as an append-only dispute object, admitted while a transfer freeze is in force and included in the transfer-safe operation set of §16.7.2 (6646-6662). It may be submitted before finalization or within the stated dispute window, and it never deletes history: 6789 says a cancellation appends SoulTransferDispute and SoulTransferResolution rather than erasing the transfer. The transfer cannot finalize while an unresolved dispute exists, because NoUnresolvedTransferDispute is a conjunct of CanFinalizeSoulTransfer at 6753, and the dispute state is committed in transfer_dispute_registry_root at 784. The submission is transfer-gated rather than authority-gated: the freeze allowlist permits it while withholding every normal-authority operation, so a frozen body submits it under the transfer gate alone.","grounds":[6789],"why":"6789 is the line that carries the name and it carries the act only as an effect of cancellation — the specification says the dispute object is appended, which presupposes a submission, and states neither the submitter nor the order. 647 shows the specification knows exactly what the object records (grounds, submitter, target agreement, time, freeze continuation) and still never narrates its submission; 6753 makes NoUnresolvedTransferDispute a conjunct of finalization, which requires an addressable dispute state to exist; 784 fixes transfer_dispute_registry_root for exactly that state. Three lines therefore use the act as a condition while no line defines the procedure.","override":"A line stating a party submitting a SoulTransferDispute — naming the submitter, the object and the moment relative to the freeze and the finalization — would make the act stated and this rule recite."}
{"kind":"RevokeStorageContribution","disposition":"stated_here","spec_name":"該当寄与を失効・減衰させる","presupposition":9744,"act":"該当寄与を失効・減衰させる","rule":"The design adopts revocation as a storage provider act under storage_provider_authorization: the provider submits a revocation naming the contribution (its StorageReservationGrant plus the receipts and audits that evidence it), admitted only against a pre-state checkpoint and a storage_audit_validity proof, progressing submitted -> accepted -> finalized, and idempotent on the operation identity tuple. The same effect is what §20.7.6 fixes for an audit failure — the contribution's credited capacity is withdrawn and decays so it no longer contributes to effective storage under §24.13's accounting. Revocation withdraws credited capacity only: it never deletes durable bytes (deletion is the separate §20.7.7 GC gate) and never retrospectively invalidates a receipt already verified, per the append-only rule that revocation advances state by a later object rather than erasing a past one.","grounds":[9744,13640],"why":"Line 9744 is the §20.7.6 audit rule: it makes audit success valid only against the object's ciphertext hash/manifest (so a failure is a genuine mismatch, never a liveness or self-declared-inventory signal) and states the failure consequence verbatim as 該当寄与を失効・減衰させる; line 13640 repeats the same sentence word for word in the storage test list, which confirms the act is a rule of the specification rather than an accident of one sentence. Both lines state the effect and thereby presuppose the act, but neither names the actor, its authority, the pre-state, the object produced, or any rejection, and no line anywhere states them; §20.7.6 is therefore the section that defines StorageAudit and not this operation, and it may not be claimed as a defining section.","override":"A line naming who revokes a storage contribution and stating the revocation procedure — for example an enumerated revoke sequence, or a sentence that the provider withdraws the capacity on an audit failure together with its pre-state and rejection codes — would make the act stated and move the row to single_step; equally, a line making the decay purely an audit result with no revoking act would collapse the operation into StorageAuditResponse."}
```

## 5. What this document does not claim

It does not claim that the derivation is right in every judgement. It claims that every row cites
the lines that establish it, that every step cites the line that states it, that every name the
specification gives is present, that every object type in the inventory has a producer, that every
reject code has a family, and that every sequence the specification states has a decomposition or
an adjudication with a line that shows why it is not a sequence. Those claims are checked on every
run. What cannot be checked is the reading, and the readings are what the campaigns below are.

**The first campaign** read every chapter in eight blocks and produced 303 findings. Folding them
in added 59 operations, 86 sequence entries and 104 joint requirements.

**The second and third** read every remaining section and audited the rows the first had left
standing. Of the 430 sections at heading level three or deeper, 121 are spanned completely by a
sequence entry, 167 partially, and 142 carry no Core Operation sequence at all — each adjudicated
with a category and a reason, and the checker fails the run if any section is neither spanned nor
adjudicated. The operations left as single-step were then put to readers one at a time; the ones
whose section does state an ordered flow were folded into entries that already existed.

**The gaps.** Of the 110 emergent entries, 81 were shown by a whole-specification search to be
already stated — each recorded in `resolved_by` with the line that states it — and 10 were
withdrawn. That left 19, each then searched a second time, adversarially, on the instruction to
falsify the claim that no line governs it. All 19 survived and each carries the rule this design
adopts, at 4.1. **A gap that survives two independent whole-specification searches is recorded as
open, and no gap is recorded as open on the strength of one reading.**

**The fourth pass tested what the composition asserts**, and this is where the document stopped
being a claim and became a repair. Each of 1583 steps was put to a reader with one question — does
this cited line state this step. **1030 said yes.** The other 553 did not, and each was then
repaired rather than counted:

| what the line was | n | what happened |
|---|---|---|
| a rule, prohibition or boundary rather than an act | 371 | folded into the contract of the operation it qualifies |
| an inventory: a schema, a field list, a formula, an endpoint list | 166 | folded, or dropped when it qualifies nothing |
| a scope statement, a lead-in, or a different act | 9 | folded or dropped |
| one act that the registry carried as two operation rows | 7 | folded into the surviving row |

The repair folded each unstated step into the contract of the operation it qualifies, or removed
it when it qualified nothing. 307 sentences moved that way in the first repair; 183 of them sit on
the steps that survive, and the rest went with the entries the fifth pass withdrew, carried into
the \u0060states\u0060 list of their adjudications so that nothing folded is lost.
**Of the 942 steps that remain, 942 are stated by their own line.** Twenty further steps were
counted twice — one act written as two steps, which the numbering check could not see — and were
removed.

**The fifth pass asked the question no per-step check can ask: is the entry a sequence at all.**
The first repair found 81 steps whose fold had nowhere to land — every step of the entry folding
into the one operation it bound — which is an entry announcing that it is not a sequence. Forty-
three entries were read on that question and **withdrawn**: 13 property definitions, 10 invariant
lists, 8 requirements, 3 computations, 3 transition statements, 3 non-sequence prose, 2 schemas and
1 prohibition list. Each is recorded with the line that shows it, a category, and a list of
what the entry *does* state, because withdrawing a decomposition must not lose what it contained.
**The concentration that prompted the reading separates nothing:** the specification writes its own
execution order at 14147 as `(1) … (13)` on one line, and that entry was kept.

**The rows, in the other direction.** Every one of the 257 rows was put to a reader with one
question — does the cited line DEFINE this operation. **144 do.** 28 were grounded on a line that
only mentioned the operation and were re-grounded on the line that states the act; 10 are interface
frames or reads; 35 are object types no operation produces; and 30 are acts the specification
**names and never states**, found by searching for the act rather than for the name. Those thirty
are not left open and they are not invented: each carries a rule at 4.2, with the string the
specification actually uses for the act and the line carrying it, and the checker requires that
string to occur on that line.

**The number to read is the one that moved.** This document invented most of its operation names,
so a search for a name proves nothing: of twenty rows a name search called undefined, seven had a
defining line when the same reader was sent to look for the act. And the correction that mattered
most was in the other direction — six operations had been placed in sequences that turned out to
be lists, because their names appeared in the lines.

The summary below is printed on every run. Exit 0 means every check passed; it does not mean the
derivation is right in every judgement, and it never did.
